microcluster/__init__.py

 1 | """MicroCluster: privacy-preserving health-signal detection and disclosure.
 2 | 
 3 | Two independent engines:
 4 | 
 5 | - ``microcluster.detection``  -- is anything unusual happening?
 6 | - ``microcluster.disclosure`` -- what, if anything, may be said, and at
 7 |   what scope?
 8 | 
 9 | See ``CLAUDE.md`` for the thesis and the decided rules.
10 | """
11 | 
12 | from . import config, detection, disclosure, engine, models
13 | 
14 | __all__ = ["config", "detection", "disclosure", "engine", "models"]

microcluster/config.py

  1 | """Policy constants and configuration for MicroCluster.
  2 | 
  3 | EVERYTHING IN THIS MODULE IS A PROJECT-DEFINED POLICY, NOT A PROVEN LAW.
  4 | 
  5 | Each value is:
  6 |   - named (nothing here is inlined into scoring code),
  7 |   - configurable (via ``DetectionConfig`` / ``DisclosureConfig``),
  8 |   - a deliberate choice, explained in the comment beside it and in
  9 |     ``CLAUDE.md``.
 10 | 
 11 | None of these is derived from an epidemiological or statistical result.
 12 | ``ceil(sqrt(n))`` is a reasonable *shape* for "evidence should scale with
 13 | the size of the claim" -- not a theorem about this problem.
 14 | """
 15 | 
 16 | from __future__ import annotations
 17 | 
 18 | from dataclasses import dataclass
 19 | 
 20 | # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
 21 | # Detection window (rule 5)
 22 | # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
 23 | 
 24 | # How recently a report must have been submitted to count as "current
 25 | # activity". Policy choice. Default 72 hours.
 26 | DETECTION_WINDOW_HOURS: int = 72
 27 | 
 28 | 
 29 | # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
 30 | # Absolute detector (rule 3, rule 4)
 31 | # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
 32 | 
 33 | # BACKGROUND_RATE: the approximate fraction of a population expected to
 34 | # submit a qualifying symptom report within ONE detection window under
 35 | # ordinary, non-outbreak conditions.
 36 | #
 37 | # THIS IS AN ILLUSTRATIVE FIGURE. IT IS NOT A CLINICAL OR EPIDEMIOLOGICAL
 38 | # ESTIMATE. It exists only to give the absolute detector a reference point
 39 | # to compare against. Before trusting the absolute detector for a real
 40 | # community, replace this with a rate measured from that community's own
 41 | # quiet-period baseline.
 42 | BACKGROUND_RATE: float = 0.02
 43 | 
 44 | # The absolute detector fires when the observed within-window report count
 45 | # reaches this multiple of the background expectation
 46 | # (BACKGROUND_RATE * declared campus population). Policy choice.
 47 | ABSOLUTE_EXCESS_RATIO: float = 1.5
 48 | 
 49 | 
 50 | # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
 51 | # Relative detector (rule 3)
 52 | # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
 53 | 
 54 | # The relative detector fires when a location's per-capita within-window
 55 | # report rate reaches this multiple of its contemporaneous peers' pooled
 56 | # per-capita rate. Policy choice.
 57 | RELATIVE_EXCESS_RATIO: float = 2.0
 58 | 
 59 | # Additive (Laplace) smoothing applied to BOTH sides of the relative
 60 | # excess ratio:
 61 | #
 62 | #   (reports_here + k) / (n_here + k)   divided by
 63 | #   (reports_control + k) / (n_control + k)
 64 | #
 65 | # Without it, a small quiet peer group drives the control rate to nearly
 66 | # zero and the ratio explodes to hundreds or infinity -- a number that
 67 | # looks precise but is dominated by division noise. k pulls both rates
 68 | # toward a neutral prior; the ratio stays finite and interpretable.
 69 | # Policy choice. Default 1 (one pseudo-report per pseudo-person).
 70 | RELATIVE_SMOOTHING: float = 1.0
 71 | 
 72 | # ...and only if that location has at least this many within-window
 73 | # reports. Without a floor, one report in a tiny group produces an
 74 | # enormous ratio and constant false positives. Policy choice.
 75 | MIN_RELATIVE_CLUSTER_REPORTS: int = 3
 76 | 
 77 | 
 78 | # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
 79 | # Detection hysteresis
 80 | # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
 81 | #
 82 | # detection.evaluate() is otherwise a pure, single-instant function: fired
 83 | # is recomputed from scratch each call, from whatever happens to be in the
 84 | # window that instant. Called once a day over a live event, that means an
 85 | # ordinary day-to-day wobble in which reports happen to fall inside a
 86 | # rolling 72h window can flip "fired" off and back on in the middle of one
 87 | # ongoing event -- not because anything really went away, but because the
 88 | # window's edge moved. Hysteresis smooths that out. It does not change what
 89 | # a single evaluation computes; it changes how a SEQUENCE of evaluations
 90 | # (threaded via ``HysteresisState``) is allowed to report "fired".
 91 | 
 92 | # Once fired, "fired" is held True for at least this many consecutive
 93 | # evaluations, even on a day where the raw relative/absolute check alone
 94 | # would say no. Policy choice.
 95 | HYSTERESIS_MIN_FIRED_DAYS: int = 3
 96 | 
 97 | # ...unless the window's qualifying report count drops to this floor or
 98 | # below, in which case the hold releases immediately regardless of the
 99 | # minimum above -- a count this low means the event is genuinely over, and
100 | # holding "fired" would describe a signal that is no longer there. Policy
101 | # choice.
102 | HYSTERESIS_RELEASE_THRESHOLD: int = 3
103 | 
104 | 
105 | # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
106 | # Disclosure -- statistical gate (rule 8)
107 | # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
108 | 
109 | # statistical threshold = max(STATISTICAL_GATE_FLOOR, ceil(sqrt(n)))
110 | #
111 | # Scales UP with declared population n: a claim about a larger population
112 | # must be backed by more reports. The floor keeps very small scopes from
113 | # clearing the statistical gate on just one or two reports.
114 | STATISTICAL_GATE_FLOOR: int = 5
115 | 
116 | 
117 | # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
118 | # Disclosure -- privacy gate (rule 8)
119 | # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
120 | 
121 | # A scope may only be disclosed if its DECLARED population is at least this
122 | # large. Small scopes are where a disclosure can re-identify the person who
123 | # reported. This bound scales DOWN with population -- it only bites on the
124 | # small scopes -- which is the opposite direction to the statistical gate,
125 | # on purpose. Policy choice. Default 20.
126 | MIN_SCOPE_POPULATION: int = 20
127 | 
128 | # ...and the qualifying report count must not exceed this fraction of the
129 | # declared population. If half of a declared group is "reporting", naming
130 | # the group tells you a lot about specific people in it. Policy choice.
131 | MAX_REPORT_FRACTION: float = 0.5
132 | 
133 | 
134 | # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
135 | # Disclosure -- scope stability across a sequence of disclosures
136 | # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
137 | #
138 | # disclosure.evaluate() is otherwise a pure, single-instant function too:
139 | # on each call it picks the finest scope passing both gates *that instant*,
140 | # with no memory of what was disclosed last time. Called once a day over a
141 | # live event, that means the named scope can wander from suite to suite as
142 | # the evidence shifts night to night. Each individual disclosure still
143 | # passes both gates on its own, but the SEQUENCE leaks far more than any
144 | # one of them does: an observer who watches it wander learns about every
145 | # group it ever names, not just one. Once a scope has been named, evaluate()
146 | # keeps naming that scope (or a coarser ancestor of it, if the exact scope
147 | # stops qualifying) unless a different scope's qualifying count clearly
148 | # outweighs it.
149 | 
150 | # How much a candidate OUTSIDE the currently disclosed scope's own lineage
151 | # (itself or its ancestors) must exceed the current scope's qualifying
152 | # count by before disclosure is allowed to switch to it. Below this margin,
153 | # disclosure stays put -- it never silently hops between sibling scopes on
154 | # a small, possibly noisy, difference in count. Policy choice.
155 | SIBLING_SWITCH_MARGIN: int = 5
156 | 
157 | 
158 | # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
159 | # Abuse mitigation (rule 11)
160 | # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
161 | 
162 | # Reports are anonymous. We therefore accept that abuse is possible and
163 | # apply only light, anonymous mitigation -- never identity-based controls,
164 | # because identity is exactly what the system exists to protect.
165 | #
166 | # These caps are enforced at INTAKE by ``microcluster.intake`` (wired into
167 | # ``engine.analyze`` as its first step). A report carries an opaque,
168 | # ephemeral ``session_id``; that is the ONLY thing the cap keys on. It is
169 | # rate limiting, not authentication: a report with no session_id is not
170 | # rate limited (there is nothing to key on), and an attacker who spreads
171 | # their reports across many sessions defeats a per-session cap outright.
172 | # ``adversarial.injection`` measures exactly what the cap does and does
173 | # not buy.
174 | SESSION_SUBMISSION_CAP: int = 3     # max accepted reports per session_id (its lifetime)
175 | DAILY_SUBMISSION_CAP: int = 10      # max accepted reports per session_id per calendar day
176 | 
177 | 
178 | # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
179 | # Configuration objects
180 | # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
181 | 
182 | 
183 | @dataclass(frozen=True)
184 | class DetectionConfig:
185 |     """Tunable inputs to the detection engine. Defaults come from the
186 |     module-level policy constants above."""
187 | 
188 |     detection_window_hours: int = DETECTION_WINDOW_HOURS
189 |     background_rate: float = BACKGROUND_RATE
190 |     absolute_excess_ratio: float = ABSOLUTE_EXCESS_RATIO
191 |     relative_excess_ratio: float = RELATIVE_EXCESS_RATIO
192 |     relative_smoothing: float = RELATIVE_SMOOTHING
193 |     min_relative_cluster_reports: int = MIN_RELATIVE_CLUSTER_REPORTS
194 |     hysteresis_min_fired_days: int = HYSTERESIS_MIN_FIRED_DAYS
195 |     hysteresis_release_threshold: int = HYSTERESIS_RELEASE_THRESHOLD
196 | 
197 | 
198 | @dataclass(frozen=True)
199 | class DisclosureConfig:
200 |     """Tunable inputs to the disclosure engine. Defaults come from the
201 |     module-level policy constants above."""
202 | 
203 |     statistical_gate_floor: int = STATISTICAL_GATE_FLOOR
204 |     min_scope_population: int = MIN_SCOPE_POPULATION
205 |     max_report_fraction: float = MAX_REPORT_FRACTION
206 |     sibling_switch_margin: int = SIBLING_SWITCH_MARGIN
207 | 
208 | 
209 | @dataclass(frozen=True)
210 | class IntakeConfig:
211 |     """Tunable inputs to the intake submission caps (rule 11). Defaults
212 |     come from the module-level policy constants above. Anonymous rate
213 |     limiting only -- see the SESSION_SUBMISSION_CAP comment."""
214 | 
215 |     session_submission_cap: int = SESSION_SUBMISSION_CAP
216 |     daily_submission_cap: int = DAILY_SUBMISSION_CAP
217 | 
218 | 
219 | DEFAULT_DETECTION_CONFIG = DetectionConfig()
220 | DEFAULT_DISCLOSURE_CONFIG = DisclosureConfig()
221 | DEFAULT_INTAKE_CONFIG = IntakeConfig()

microcluster/demo.py

  1 | """Print the six reference scenarios and their evaluation tables.
  2 | 
  3 |     python -m microcluster.demo
  4 | 
  5 | This is a read-only illustration of the two engines. It builds the same
  6 | fixtures the tests use.
  7 | """
  8 | 
  9 | from __future__ import annotations
 10 | 
 11 | import sys
 12 | from pathlib import Path
 13 | 
 14 | sys.path.insert(0, str(Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent))
 15 | 
 16 | from microcluster.disclosure import DisclosureResult  # noqa: E402
 17 | from microcluster.engine import AnalysisResult, analyze  # noqa: E402
 18 | from microcluster.models import Category  # noqa: E402
 19 | from tests.fixtures import (  # noqa: E402
 20 |     NOW,
 21 |     reports,
 22 |     small_registry_floor18,
 23 |     standard_registry,
 24 | )
 25 | 
 26 | 
 27 | def _fmt_detection(result: AnalysisResult) -> str:
 28 |     d = result.detection
 29 |     f = d.findings
 30 |     fired = []
 31 |     if d.relative_detector_fired:
 32 |         fired.append("RELATIVE")
 33 |     if d.absolute_detector_fired:
 34 |         fired.append("ABSOLUTE")
 35 |     lines = [
 36 |         f"  detection fired : {d.fired}  ({' + '.join(fired) if fired else 'neither detector'})",
 37 |         f"  findings        : relative_excess_ratio = {f.relative_excess_ratio:.2f}"
 38 |         f" (hotspot {d.hotspot_location_id})",
 39 |         f"                    count_within_window   = {f.count_within_window}"
 40 |         f"  (absolute expectation {d.absolute_expected:.1f})",
 41 |         f"                    count_sharing_category= {f.count_sharing_category}"
 42 |         f"  (category {d.dominant_category.value if d.dominant_category else '-'})",
 43 |     ]
 44 |     return "\n".join(lines)
 45 | 
 46 | 
 47 | def _fmt_table(disc: DisclosureResult) -> str:
 48 |     if not disc.evaluations:
 49 |         return "  (disclosure engine did not run -- nothing detected)"
 50 |     header = (
 51 |         f"  {'scope':<26} {'lvl':<8} {'n':>5} {'q':>4} "
 52 |         f"{'stat>=':>7} {'stat':>5} {'n>=20':>6} {'frac':>6} {'frac<=':>7} "
 53 |         f"{'priv':>5} {'DISCLOSE':>9}"
 54 |     )
 55 |     rows = [header, "  " + "-" * (len(header) - 2)]
 56 |     for ev in disc.evaluations:
 57 |         label = ev.scope_label if len(ev.scope_label) <= 26 else ev.scope_label[:23] + "..."
 58 |         rows.append(
 59 |             f"  {label:<26} {ev.scope_level.name:<8} {ev.population:>5} "
 60 |             f"{ev.qualifying_reports:>4} {ev.statistical_threshold:>7} "
 61 |             f"{('PASS' if ev.statistical_pass else 'FAIL'):>5} "
 62 |             f"{('PASS' if ev.min_population_pass else 'FAIL'):>6} "
 63 |             f"{ev.report_fraction:>6.2f} "
 64 |             f"{('PASS' if ev.report_fraction_pass else 'FAIL'):>7} "
 65 |             f"{('PASS' if ev.privacy_pass else 'FAIL'):>5} "
 66 |             f"{('*SELECTED*' if ev.selected else ('eligible' if ev.disclosure_eligible else 'no')):>9}"
 67 |         )
 68 |     rows.append("")
 69 |     for ev in disc.evaluations:
 70 |         rows.append(f"    {ev.scope_label}: {ev.disclosure_reason}")
 71 |     return "\n".join(rows)
 72 | 
 73 | 
 74 | def _scenario(title: str, result: AnalysisResult) -> None:
 75 |     print("=" * 78)
 76 |     print(title)
 77 |     print("=" * 78)
 78 |     print(_fmt_detection(result))
 79 |     print()
 80 |     print(_fmt_table(result.disclosure))
 81 |     disclosed = result.disclosure.disclosed()
 82 |     print()
 83 |     if disclosed is None:
 84 |         print("  >> DISCLOSED: nothing")
 85 |     else:
 86 |         print(f"  >> DISCLOSED: {disclosed.scope_label}  (n={disclosed.population}, "
 87 |               f"{disclosed.qualifying_reports} qualifying reports)")
 88 |     print()
 89 | 
 90 | 
 91 | def main() -> None:
 92 |     reg = standard_registry()
 93 | 
 94 |     # 1. Localized cluster -- relative fires, absolute does not.
 95 |     #    All reports at suite level (detection requires one scope level).
 96 |     localized = (
 97 |         reports("S1", 9, category=Category.RESPIRATORY)
 98 |         + reports("S2", 2, category=Category.GENERAL)
 99 |         + reports("S3", 1, category=Category.GASTROINTESTINAL)
100 |         + reports("S4", 1, category=Category.OTHER)
101 |         + reports("S5", 1, category=Category.GENERAL)
102 |         + reports("S3", 4, category=Category.RESPIRATORY, hours_ago=400)  # outside window
103 |     )
104 |     _scenario("SCENARIO 1  Localized cluster (relative fires, absolute does not)",
105 |               analyze(localized, reg, now=NOW))
106 | 
107 |     # 2. Campus-wide rise -- absolute fires, relative does not.
108 |     campus_wide = []
109 |     for b in ("B1", "B2", "B3", "B4", "B5"):
110 |         campus_wide += reports(b, 14, category=Category.RESPIRATORY)
111 |     _scenario("SCENARIO 2  Campus-wide rise (absolute fires, relative does not)",
112 |               analyze(campus_wide, reg, now=NOW))
113 | 
114 |     # 3. Scattered noise -- neither fires. Uniform building level.
115 |     scattered = (
116 |         reports("B1", 3, category=Category.GENERAL)
117 |         + reports("B2", 3, category=Category.RESPIRATORY)
118 |         + reports("B3", 3, category=Category.GASTROINTESTINAL)
119 |         + reports("B4", 3, category=Category.OTHER)
120 |         + reports("B5", 3, category=Category.GENERAL)
121 |     )
122 |     _scenario("SCENARIO 3  Scattered noise (neither detector fires)",
123 |               analyze(scattered, reg, now=NOW))
124 | 
125 |     # 4. Overclaim refusal -- 7 reports, campus n=2000 -> statistical FAIL.
126 |     overclaim = reports("B1", 7, category=Category.RESPIRATORY)
127 |     _scenario("SCENARIO 4  Overclaim refusal (7 reports vs campus n=2000)",
128 |               analyze(overclaim, reg, now=NOW))
129 | 
130 |     # 5. Privacy refusal -- 5 reports, floor n=18 -> statistical PASS, privacy FAIL.
131 |     small = small_registry_floor18()
132 |     privacy = reports("F", 5, category=Category.RESPIRATORY)
133 |     _scenario("SCENARIO 5  Privacy refusal (5 reports, floor n=18 -> coarser scope)",
134 |               analyze(privacy, small, now=NOW))
135 | 
136 |     # 6. Both gates across populations -- see tests/test_disclosure.py for the
137 |     #    full parametric table; here we show the n=40 case passing both.
138 |     from tests.fixtures import single_scope_registry
139 | 
140 |     across = reports("C", 8, category=Category.RESPIRATORY)
141 |     _scenario("SCENARIO 6  Both gates, n=40 (statistical threshold 7, privacy ok)",
142 |               analyze(across, single_scope_registry(40), now=NOW))
143 | 
144 |     # 7. Roster refusal -- 15 reports in a declared group of n=25: passes
145 |     #    the statistical gate and the population minimum, refused because
146 |     #    15/25 = 0.60 exceeds MAX_REPORT_FRACTION.
147 |     from tests.fixtures import roster_registry
148 | 
149 |     roster = reports("G", 15, category=Category.RESPIRATORY)
150 |     _scenario("SCENARIO 7  Roster refusal (15 reports, group n=25 -> fraction 0.60)",
151 |               analyze(roster, roster_registry(), now=NOW))
152 | 
153 | 
154 | if __name__ == "__main__":
155 |     main()

microcluster/detection.py

  1 | """Detection engine: is anything unusual happening?
  2 | 
  3 | Two independent detectors (rule 3), either of which can fire:
  4 | 
  5 |   * relative -- a location unusual versus its contemporaneous peers
  6 |   * absolute -- overall activity unusual versus a configured background
  7 |     rate (``config.BACKGROUND_RATE``, rule 4)
  8 | 
  9 | The engine reports three plain findings and never combines them into a
 10 | composite score (rule 6):
 11 | 
 12 |   * ``relative_excess_ratio``
 13 |   * ``count_within_window``
 14 |   * ``count_sharing_category``
 15 | 
 16 | Both detectors are scored on the same subset -- within-window reports in
 17 | the *dominant category* -- so that whatever trips a detector is exactly
 18 | what the disclosure engine goes on to describe, never a superset of it.
 19 | 
 20 | Its other job is to hand the disclosure engine an *aggregate* view --
 21 | per-scope qualifying report counts -- so that the disclosure engine never
 22 | touches individual records (rule 10).
 23 | 
 24 | All reports passed to :func:`evaluate` must resolve to scopes at a single
 25 | ``ScopeLevel``; see that function's docstring.
 26 | 
 27 | A single call to :func:`evaluate` is still a pure, single-instant
 28 | computation -- ``raw_fired`` is recomputed from nothing but this call's
 29 | reports. ``fired`` (the field disclosure and everything downstream acts
 30 | on) additionally applies HYSTERESIS across a *sequence* of calls, threaded
 31 | explicitly via ``HysteresisState`` (see that class and
 32 | ``config.HYSTERESIS_MIN_FIRED_DAYS`` / ``HYSTERESIS_RELEASE_THRESHOLD``).
 33 | Passing no state (the default) makes a single call behave exactly as if
 34 | hysteresis did not exist.
 35 | """
 36 | 
 37 | from __future__ import annotations
 38 | 
 39 | from collections import Counter
 40 | from dataclasses import dataclass
 41 | from datetime import datetime, timedelta, timezone
 42 | 
 43 | from .config import DEFAULT_DETECTION_CONFIG, DetectionConfig
 44 | from .models import Category, Report, ScopeLevel, ScopeRegistry
 45 | 
 46 | __all__ = [
 47 |     "DetectionFindings",
 48 |     "DetectionResult",
 49 |     "HysteresisState",
 50 |     "evaluate",
 51 | ]
 52 | 
 53 | 
 54 | @dataclass(frozen=True)
 55 | class DetectionFindings:
 56 |     """The three findings, reported plainly and separately (rule 6)."""
 57 | 
 58 |     relative_excess_ratio: float
 59 |     count_within_window: int
 60 |     count_sharing_category: int
 61 | 
 62 | 
 63 | @dataclass(frozen=True)
 64 | class HysteresisState:
 65 |     """Carries hysteresis across a SEQUENCE of :func:`evaluate` calls (one
 66 |     per day, say). Immutable: each call returns the next state as part of
 67 |     its :class:`DetectionResult`; the caller threads it into the following
 68 |     call. The default, ``HysteresisState()``, is "not currently active" --
 69 |     passing it (or nothing) makes hysteresis a no-op.
 70 |     """
 71 | 
 72 |     active: bool = False
 73 |     consecutive_days: int = 0
 74 | 
 75 | 
 76 | @dataclass(frozen=True)
 77 | class DetectionResult:
 78 |     fired: bool
 79 |     raw_fired: bool
 80 |     relative_detector_fired: bool
 81 |     absolute_detector_fired: bool
 82 |     findings: DetectionFindings
 83 |     hysteresis_state: HysteresisState
 84 | 
 85 |     # Context for the findings and for disclosure.
 86 |     dominant_category: Category | None
 87 |     hotspot_location_id: str | None
 88 |     absolute_expected: float
 89 |     window_start: datetime
 90 |     window_end: datetime
 91 | 
 92 |     # Aggregate handed to the disclosure engine (rule 10): for every scope
 93 |     # that contains at least one qualifying report, how many qualifying
 94 |     # reports it contains. "Qualifying" == within the detection window and
 95 |     # sharing the dominant category. Both detectors are scored on the same
 96 |     # dominant-category subset, so whatever fires is what this table (and
 97 |     # therefore the disclosure) describes.
 98 |     qualifying_scope_counts: dict[str, int]
 99 | 
100 | 
101 | def _now(now: datetime | None) -> datetime:
102 |     return now if now is not None else datetime.now(timezone.utc)
103 | 
104 | 
105 | def _within_window(
106 |     reports: list[Report], window_start: datetime, window_end: datetime
107 | ) -> list[Report]:
108 |     return [r for r in reports if window_start <= r.submitted_at <= window_end]
109 | 
110 | 
111 | def _dominant_category(window_reports: list[Report]) -> Category | None:
112 |     """The category with the most within-window reports. Ties broken
113 |     deterministically by category name so the result never depends on
114 |     input ordering."""
115 |     if not window_reports:
116 |         return None
117 |     counts = Counter(r.category for r in window_reports)
118 |     top = max(counts.values())
119 |     return sorted((c for c, n in counts.items() if n == top), key=lambda c: c.value)[0]
120 | 
121 | 
122 | def _run_relative_detector(
123 |     window_reports: list[Report],
124 |     registry: ScopeRegistry,
125 |     config: DetectionConfig,
126 |     dominant_category: Category | None,
127 | ) -> tuple[bool, float, str | None]:
128 |     """Compare each reporting location's per-capita rate against the pooled
129 |     per-capita rate of everyone else in the campus (its contemporaneous
130 |     peers). Returns (fired, excess_ratio_of_hotspot, hotspot_location_id).
131 | 
132 |     The excess ratio is computed on TOTAL within-window volume at each
133 |     location -- "is this location busier than its peers" is a volume
134 |     question. The minimum-cluster-size gate
135 |     (``config.min_relative_cluster_reports``), however, counts only the
136 |     hotspot's reports **in the dominant category**. A location whose extra
137 |     volume is really three unrelated illnesses therefore does not clear the
138 |     gate: what fires is what the disclosure engine will describe.
139 | 
140 |     Both rates are additively smoothed by ``config.relative_smoothing`` so
141 |     a quiet peer group cannot drive the ratio to infinity (see
142 |     ``config.RELATIVE_SMOOTHING``).
143 | 
144 |     Assumes every report resolves to a scope at the SAME hierarchy level
145 |     (``evaluate`` enforces this): peer population is
146 |     ``campus_population - location_population``, which is only correct when
147 |     report locations do not nest inside one another.
148 |     """
149 |     total = len(window_reports)
150 |     campus_population = registry.campus_population()
151 |     if total == 0:
152 |         return (False, 0.0, None)
153 | 
154 |     per_location = Counter(r.location_id for r in window_reports)
155 |     per_location_dominant: Counter[str] = Counter(
156 |         r.location_id for r in window_reports if r.category == dominant_category
157 |     )
158 |     k = config.relative_smoothing
159 | 
160 |     best_ratio = 0.0
161 |     hotspot: str | None = None
162 |     hotspot_dominant_count = 0
163 | 
164 |     for location_id, loc_count in per_location.items():
165 |         loc_population = registry.get(location_id).population
166 |         peer_population = campus_population - loc_population
167 |         peer_count = total - loc_count
168 |         if peer_population <= 0:
169 |             # This location IS the whole comparison set; there are no peers
170 |             # to be unusual against. Skip it for the relative detector.
171 |             continue
172 | 
173 |         # Additively smoothed rates on both sides (rule: named constant).
174 |         loc_rate = (loc_count + k) / (loc_population + k)
175 |         peer_rate = (peer_count + k) / (peer_population + k)
176 |         ratio = loc_rate / peer_rate
177 | 
178 |         if ratio > best_ratio:
179 |             best_ratio = ratio
180 |             hotspot = location_id
181 |             hotspot_dominant_count = per_location_dominant[location_id]
182 | 
183 |     fired = (
184 |         hotspot is not None
185 |         and best_ratio >= config.relative_excess_ratio
186 |         and hotspot_dominant_count >= config.min_relative_cluster_reports
187 |     )
188 |     return (fired, best_ratio, hotspot)
189 | 
190 | 
191 | def _run_absolute_detector(
192 |     window_reports: list[Report],
193 |     registry: ScopeRegistry,
194 |     config: DetectionConfig,
195 |     dominant_category: Category | None,
196 | ) -> tuple[bool, float]:
197 |     """Compare the within-window count of DOMINANT-CATEGORY reports against
198 |     the background expectation for the whole declared campus population.
199 |     Returns (fired, expected_count).
200 | 
201 |     Only dominant-category reports are counted. A spike built from several
202 |     unrelated illnesses therefore cannot fire this detector and then leave
203 |     the disclosure engine describing only one category's slice of it -- the
204 |     thing that fires is the thing that gets described. If campus-wide
205 |     elevation across mixed categories is worth flagging, it should be an
206 |     explicit, separately reported signal, not a side effect of this one.
207 | 
208 |     ``expected`` is still ``BACKGROUND_RATE * campus_population``, the
209 |     whole-population background rather than a per-category one, which makes
210 |     this a deliberately conservative test. Give ``BACKGROUND_RATE`` a
211 |     per-category value when tuning it against real baseline data.
212 |     """
213 |     expected = config.background_rate * registry.campus_population()
214 |     observed = sum(1 for r in window_reports if r.category == dominant_category)
215 |     fired = observed >= expected * config.absolute_excess_ratio
216 |     return (fired, expected)
217 | 
218 | 
219 | def _qualifying_scope_counts(
220 |     window_reports: list[Report],
221 |     registry: ScopeRegistry,
222 |     dominant_category: Category | None,
223 | ) -> dict[str, int]:
224 |     """For each scope containing at least one qualifying report, the count
225 |     of qualifying reports in its subtree. Qualifying == within window and
226 |     sharing the dominant category."""
227 |     counts: Counter[str] = Counter()
228 |     if dominant_category is None:
229 |         return {}
230 |     for report in window_reports:
231 |         if report.category != dominant_category:
232 |             continue
233 |         for scope in registry.chain_to_root(report.location_id):
234 |             counts[scope.id] += 1
235 |     return dict(counts)
236 | 
237 | 
238 | def _apply_hysteresis(
239 |     raw_fired: bool,
240 |     count_within_window: int,
241 |     prior: HysteresisState,
242 |     config: DetectionConfig,
243 | ) -> tuple[bool, HysteresisState]:
244 |     """Turn this instant's raw fired/not-fired into a hysteresis-aware
245 |     ``fired`` plus the state to pass into the next call.
246 | 
247 |     Rules (config.HYSTERESIS_MIN_FIRED_DAYS / HYSTERESIS_RELEASE_THRESHOLD):
248 | 
249 |     * Raw fired -> always fired; the active streak continues (or starts).
250 |     * Raw not fired, and not currently active -> stays not fired. Nothing
251 |       to hold.
252 |     * Raw not fired, but currently active -> HOLD fired True, UNLESS
253 |       either the window has clearly emptied out (count_within_window at or
254 |       below the release threshold: release immediately) or the minimum
255 |       hold duration has already been served (release on schedule).
256 |     """
257 |     if raw_fired:
258 |         streak = prior.consecutive_days + 1 if prior.active else 1
259 |         return True, HysteresisState(active=True, consecutive_days=streak)
260 | 
261 |     if not prior.active:
262 |         return False, HysteresisState(active=False, consecutive_days=0)
263 | 
264 |     if count_within_window <= config.hysteresis_release_threshold:
265 |         return False, HysteresisState(active=False, consecutive_days=0)
266 | 
267 |     if prior.consecutive_days < config.hysteresis_min_fired_days:
268 |         return True, HysteresisState(
269 |             active=True, consecutive_days=prior.consecutive_days + 1
270 |         )
271 | 
272 |     return False, HysteresisState(active=False, consecutive_days=0)
273 | 
274 | 
275 | def evaluate(
276 |     reports: list[Report],
277 |     registry: ScopeRegistry,
278 |     *,
279 |     now: datetime | None = None,
280 |     config: DetectionConfig = DEFAULT_DETECTION_CONFIG,
281 |     hysteresis_state: HysteresisState | None = None,
282 | ) -> DetectionResult:
283 |     """Run both detectors and assemble the findings.
284 | 
285 |     ``now`` defaults to the current UTC time; pass it explicitly for
286 |     deterministic evaluation.
287 | 
288 |     **Mixed-level assumption (enforced).** Every report must resolve to a
289 |     scope at the *same* ``ScopeLevel``. The relative detector derives a
290 |     location's peer population as ``campus_population -
291 |     location_population``; that subtraction is only correct when report
292 |     locations are siblings, not nested (a floor inside a building it also
293 |     receives reports for would be double-counted). If reports arrive at
294 |     more than one level this function raises ``ValueError`` -- normalise
295 |     them to a single level upstream. Handling true nesting is left for a
296 |     later change.
297 | 
298 |     ``hysteresis_state`` is the ``HysteresisState`` returned by the
299 |     PREVIOUS call in a sequence (e.g. yesterday's); omit it (or pass
300 |     ``None``) for a one-off evaluation, which is equivalent to always
301 |     starting "not active". The next state to thread into the following
302 |     call is returned on the result as ``hysteresis_state``.
303 |     """
304 |     window_end = _now(now)
305 |     window_start = window_end - timedelta(hours=config.detection_window_hours)
306 | 
307 |     report_levels: set[ScopeLevel] = set()
308 |     for report in reports:
309 |         # ``registry.get`` fails loudly on an unknown location; we also
310 |         # collect the scope level of every report here.
311 |         report_levels.add(registry.get(report.location_id).level)
312 |     if len(report_levels) > 1:
313 |         raise ValueError(
314 |             "detection.evaluate requires every report to be filed at the "
315 |             "same scope level; got "
316 |             f"{sorted(level.name for level in report_levels)}. The relative "
317 |             "detector computes peer population as campus_population minus the "
318 |             "reporting location's population, which is only correct when "
319 |             "report locations do not nest. Normalise reports to one level "
320 |             "before calling."
321 |         )
322 | 
323 |     window_reports = _within_window(reports, window_start, window_end)
324 | 
325 |     dominant = _dominant_category(window_reports)
326 |     count_within_window = len(window_reports)
327 |     count_sharing_category = (
328 |         sum(1 for r in window_reports if r.category == dominant) if dominant else 0
329 |     )
330 | 
331 |     relative_fired, excess_ratio, hotspot = _run_relative_detector(
332 |         window_reports, registry, config, dominant
333 |     )
334 |     absolute_fired, expected = _run_absolute_detector(
335 |         window_reports, registry, config, dominant
336 |     )
337 | 
338 |     findings = DetectionFindings(
339 |         relative_excess_ratio=excess_ratio,
340 |         count_within_window=count_within_window,
341 |         count_sharing_category=count_sharing_category,
342 |     )
343 | 
344 |     raw_fired = relative_fired or absolute_fired
345 |     fired, next_hysteresis_state = _apply_hysteresis(
346 |         raw_fired, count_within_window, hysteresis_state or HysteresisState(), config
347 |     )
348 | 
349 |     return DetectionResult(
350 |         fired=fired,
351 |         raw_fired=raw_fired,
352 |         relative_detector_fired=relative_fired,
353 |         absolute_detector_fired=absolute_fired,
354 |         findings=findings,
355 |         hysteresis_state=next_hysteresis_state,
356 |         dominant_category=dominant,
357 |         hotspot_location_id=hotspot,
358 |         absolute_expected=expected,
359 |         window_start=window_start,
360 |         window_end=window_end,
361 |         qualifying_scope_counts=_qualifying_scope_counts(
362 |             window_reports, registry, dominant
363 |         ),
364 |     )

microcluster/disclosure.py

  1 | """Disclosure engine: what, if anything, may be said, and at what scope?
  2 | 
  3 | Runs after detection (rule 10). It is handed an *aggregate* table --
  4 | per-scope qualifying report counts -- plus the scope registry. It never
  5 | sees an individual record.
  6 | 
  7 | Two separate gates (rule 8), evaluated independently, never merged into
  8 | one number:
  9 | 
 10 |   * statistical gate: qualifying >= max(floor, ceil(sqrt(n)))  -- up with n
 11 |   * privacy gate:     n >= MIN_SCOPE_POPULATION
 12 |                       AND qualifying / n <= MAX_REPORT_FRACTION -- down with n
 13 | 
 14 | Both must pass for a scope to be disclosure-eligible. The finest eligible
 15 | scope is selected (rule 9). Every candidate scope -- winner and rejected
 16 | alike -- gets a complete ``ScopeEvaluation`` record (rule 12), and the
 17 | winner is picked *from that table*, never on a separate path.
 18 | 
 19 | A single call to :func:`evaluate` is still exactly that: one instant's
 20 | gate table. Called in a SEQUENCE (e.g. once a day), it additionally offers
 21 | SCOPE STABILITY -- pass the previous call's winning scope id as
 22 | ``prior_disclosed_scope_id`` and evaluate() prefers to keep disclosing that
 23 | scope, or a coarser ancestor of it, rather than hopping to a different
 24 | scope on a small night-to-night difference in count (see
 25 | ``config.SIBLING_SWITCH_MARGIN``). Passing no prior id (the default) makes
 26 | a single call behave exactly as if stability did not exist.
 27 | """
 28 | 
 29 | from __future__ import annotations
 30 | 
 31 | import math
 32 | from dataclasses import dataclass
 33 | 
 34 | from .config import DEFAULT_DISCLOSURE_CONFIG, DisclosureConfig
 35 | from .models import ScopeLevel, ScopeRegistry
 36 | 
 37 | __all__ = [
 38 |     "ScopeEvaluation",
 39 |     "DisclosureResult",
 40 |     "statistical_threshold",
 41 |     "evaluate",
 42 | ]
 43 | 
 44 | 
 45 | def _ceil_sqrt(n: int) -> int:
 46 |     """ceil(sqrt(n)) computed in integers, so perfect squares are exact
 47 |     (float sqrt(x*x) can land just under x)."""
 48 |     root = math.isqrt(n)
 49 |     return root if root * root == n else root + 1
 50 | 
 51 | 
 52 | def statistical_threshold(n: int, config: DisclosureConfig = DEFAULT_DISCLOSURE_CONFIG) -> int:
 53 |     """The statistical gate threshold for a declared population ``n``:
 54 | 
 55 |         max(STATISTICAL_GATE_FLOOR, ceil(sqrt(n)))
 56 | 
 57 |     Scales up with n -- a bigger claim needs more evidence (rule 8). This
 58 |     is a policy shape, not a proven law (see ``config.py``).
 59 |     """
 60 |     return max(config.statistical_gate_floor, _ceil_sqrt(n))
 61 | 
 62 | 
 63 | @dataclass(frozen=True)
 64 | class ScopeEvaluation:
 65 |     """A complete evaluation record for one candidate scope (rule 12).
 66 | 
 67 |     A future UI renders these directly and must never recompute a gate.
 68 |     """
 69 | 
 70 |     scope_id: str
 71 |     scope_label: str
 72 |     scope_level: ScopeLevel
 73 |     population: int
 74 |     qualifying_reports: int
 75 | 
 76 |     # Statistical gate.
 77 |     statistical_threshold: int
 78 |     statistical_pass: bool
 79 | 
 80 |     # Privacy gate (two conditions, both required).
 81 |     min_scope_population: int
 82 |     min_population_pass: bool
 83 |     report_fraction: float
 84 |     max_report_fraction: float
 85 |     report_fraction_pass: bool
 86 |     privacy_pass: bool
 87 | 
 88 |     # Outcome.
 89 |     disclosure_eligible: bool
 90 |     selected: bool
 91 |     disclosure_reason: str
 92 | 
 93 | 
 94 | @dataclass(frozen=True)
 95 | class DisclosureResult:
 96 |     # Ordered finest scope first. Includes rejected scopes (rule 12).
 97 |     evaluations: tuple[ScopeEvaluation, ...]
 98 |     disclosed_scope_id: str | None
 99 | 
100 |     def disclosed(self) -> ScopeEvaluation | None:
101 |         for ev in self.evaluations:
102 |             if ev.selected:
103 |                 return ev
104 |         return None
105 | 
106 | 
107 | def _fineness_key(ev_scope_level: ScopeLevel, population: int, scope_id: str):
108 |     """Sort key: finest first. Deeper level wins; then smaller declared
109 |     population; then scope id for a total, stable order."""
110 |     return (-int(ev_scope_level), population, scope_id)
111 | 
112 | 
113 | def _reason(
114 |     *,
115 |     selected: bool,
116 |     eligible: bool,
117 |     statistical_pass: bool,
118 |     min_population_pass: bool,
119 |     report_fraction_pass: bool,
120 |     qualifying: int,
121 |     threshold: int,
122 |     n: int,
123 |     min_pop: int,
124 |     fraction: float,
125 |     max_fraction: float,
126 |     stability_note: str | None = None,
127 | ) -> str:
128 |     if selected:
129 |         return stability_note or "Disclosed: finest scope passing both the statistical and privacy gates."
130 |     if eligible:
131 |         return stability_note or "Eligible: passes both gates, but a finer scope was disclosed."
132 | 
133 |     fails: list[str] = []
134 |     if not statistical_pass:
135 |         fails.append(
136 |             f"statistical gate failed ({qualifying} qualifying reports < "
137 |             f"required {threshold} for n={n})"
138 |         )
139 |     if not min_population_pass:
140 |         fails.append(
141 |             f"population n={n} below minimum privacy scope ({min_pop})"
142 |         )
143 |     if not report_fraction_pass:
144 |         fails.append(
145 |             f"report fraction {fraction:.2f} exceeds maximum {max_fraction:.2f} "
146 |             f"(disclosure would name so large a share of the group that the "
147 |             f"statement is effectively a roster)"
148 |         )
149 |     return "Rejected: " + "; ".join(fails)
150 | 
151 | 
152 | def _stable_selection(
153 |     gate_rows: list[dict],
154 |     registry: ScopeRegistry,
155 |     prior_disclosed_scope_id: str | None,
156 |     config: DisclosureConfig,
157 | ) -> tuple[str | None, dict[str, str]]:
158 |     """Pick the winner with scope stability applied, and any reason-text
159 |     overrides that result from it.
160 | 
161 |     Returns ``(selected_id, stability_notes)``. When there is no usable
162 |     prior scope this round, ``stability_notes`` is empty and
163 |     ``selected_id`` is exactly the plain finest-eligible pick -- a single
164 |     call with no ``prior_disclosed_scope_id`` is unaffected by any of this.
165 |     """
166 |     by_id = {row["scope"].id: row for row in gate_rows}
167 |     eligible_ids = {sid for sid, row in by_id.items() if row["eligible"]}
168 |     finest_eligible_id = next(
169 |         (row["scope"].id for row in gate_rows if row["eligible"]), None
170 |     )
171 | 
172 |     lineage_ids: set[str] = set()
173 |     if prior_disclosed_scope_id is not None and prior_disclosed_scope_id in by_id:
174 |         lineage_ids = {
175 |             scope.id
176 |             for scope in registry.chain_to_root(prior_disclosed_scope_id)
177 |             if scope.id in by_id
178 |         }
179 |     lineage_eligible_ids = [sid for sid in lineage_ids if sid in eligible_ids]
180 | 
181 |     if not lineage_eligible_ids:
182 |         # No usable incumbent (or ancestor of one) this round: plain
183 |         # finest-eligible selection, identical to having no stability rule.
184 |         return finest_eligible_id, {}
185 | 
186 |     stable_id = min(
187 |         lineage_eligible_ids,
188 |         key=lambda sid: _fineness_key(
189 |             by_id[sid]["scope"].level, by_id[sid]["scope"].population, sid
190 |         ),
191 |     )
192 |     stable_count = by_id[stable_id]["qualifying"]
193 |     margin = config.sibling_switch_margin
194 | 
195 |     challengers = sorted(
196 |         (
197 |             row
198 |             for sid, row in by_id.items()
199 |             if row["eligible"] and sid not in lineage_ids and row["qualifying"] > stable_count + margin
200 |         ),
201 |         key=lambda row: _fineness_key(row["scope"].level, row["scope"].population, row["scope"].id),
202 |     )
203 | 
204 |     notes: dict[str, str] = {}
205 |     if challengers:
206 |         winner = challengers[0]
207 |         selected_id = winner["scope"].id
208 |         notes[selected_id] = (
209 |             f"Disclosed: qualifying count ({winner['qualifying']}) exceeded the "
210 |             f"previously disclosed scope's ({stable_count}) by more than the "
211 |             f"switch margin ({margin}); disclosure moved here."
212 |         )
213 |     else:
214 |         selected_id = stable_id
215 |         if stable_id == prior_disclosed_scope_id:
216 |             notes[selected_id] = (
217 |                 "Disclosed: kept disclosing the same scope as before for "
218 |                 f"stability; no other scope exceeded it by the switch margin "
219 |                 f"({margin})."
220 |             )
221 |         else:
222 |             notes[selected_id] = (
223 |                 "Disclosed: the previously disclosed scope no longer "
224 |                 "qualifies; fell back to a coarser ancestor of it for "
225 |                 "stability rather than switching to a different scope."
226 |             )
227 | 
228 |     # Annotate scopes that lost specifically to the margin, or to a finer
229 |     # scope within the same kept lineage.
230 |     for sid, row in by_id.items():
231 |         if sid == selected_id or not row["eligible"]:
232 |             continue
233 |         if sid in lineage_ids:
234 |             notes[sid] = (
235 |                 "Eligible: passes both gates, but a finer scope in the same "
236 |                 "disclosure lineage was selected instead."
237 |             )
238 |         elif row["qualifying"] <= stable_count + margin:
239 |             notes[sid] = (
240 |                 f"Eligible: passes both gates, but qualifying count "
241 |                 f"({row['qualifying']}) did not exceed the disclosed scope's "
242 |                 f"({stable_count}) by the required switch margin ({margin}); "
243 |                 f"kept the existing disclosure instead of switching here."
244 |             )
245 |         # else: eligible, outside the lineage, cleared the margin, but a
246 |         # finer challenger won -- falls through to the default "a finer
247 |         # scope was disclosed" reason.
248 | 
249 |     return selected_id, notes
250 | 
251 | 
252 | def evaluate(
253 |     registry: ScopeRegistry,
254 |     qualifying_scope_counts: dict[str, int],
255 |     *,
256 |     config: DisclosureConfig = DEFAULT_DISCLOSURE_CONFIG,
257 |     prior_disclosed_scope_id: str | None = None,
258 | ) -> DisclosureResult:
259 |     """Evaluate both gates for every candidate scope and select a winner.
260 | 
261 |     ``qualifying_scope_counts`` maps a scope id to the number of qualifying
262 |     reports in that scope's subtree (produced by the detection engine).
263 |     Only scopes appearing as keys are candidates.
264 | 
265 |     Without ``prior_disclosed_scope_id``, the winner is simply the finest
266 |     eligible scope (rule 9). With it, scope stability applies: the winner
267 |     stays within the prior scope's own lineage (itself or a coarser
268 |     ancestor of it) unless some other scope's qualifying count exceeds it
269 |     by more than ``config.sibling_switch_margin`` -- see the module
270 |     docstring.
271 |     """
272 |     candidates = sorted(
273 |         qualifying_scope_counts.keys(),
274 |         key=lambda sid: _fineness_key(
275 |             registry.get(sid).level, registry.get(sid).population, sid
276 |         ),
277 |     )
278 | 
279 |     # First pass: gate results, no selection yet.
280 |     gate_rows: list[dict] = []
281 |     for scope_id in candidates:
282 |         scope = registry.get(scope_id)
283 |         n = scope.population
284 |         qualifying = qualifying_scope_counts[scope_id]
285 | 
286 |         threshold = statistical_threshold(n, config)
287 |         statistical_pass = qualifying >= threshold
288 | 
289 |         min_population_pass = n >= config.min_scope_population
290 |         fraction = qualifying / n
291 |         report_fraction_pass = fraction <= config.max_report_fraction
292 |         privacy_pass = min_population_pass and report_fraction_pass
293 | 
294 |         gate_rows.append(
295 |             {
296 |                 "scope": scope,
297 |                 "qualifying": qualifying,
298 |                 "threshold": threshold,
299 |                 "statistical_pass": statistical_pass,
300 |                 "min_population_pass": min_population_pass,
301 |                 "fraction": fraction,
302 |                 "report_fraction_pass": report_fraction_pass,
303 |                 "privacy_pass": privacy_pass,
304 |                 "eligible": statistical_pass and privacy_pass,
305 |             }
306 |         )
307 | 
308 |     selected_id, stability_notes = _stable_selection(
309 |         gate_rows, registry, prior_disclosed_scope_id, config
310 |     )
311 | 
312 |     evaluations = tuple(
313 |         ScopeEvaluation(
314 |             scope_id=row["scope"].id,
315 |             scope_label=row["scope"].label,
316 |             scope_level=row["scope"].level,
317 |             population=row["scope"].population,
318 |             qualifying_reports=row["qualifying"],
319 |             statistical_threshold=row["threshold"],
320 |             statistical_pass=row["statistical_pass"],
321 |             min_scope_population=config.min_scope_population,
322 |             min_population_pass=row["min_population_pass"],
323 |             report_fraction=row["fraction"],
324 |             max_report_fraction=config.max_report_fraction,
325 |             report_fraction_pass=row["report_fraction_pass"],
326 |             privacy_pass=row["privacy_pass"],
327 |             disclosure_eligible=row["eligible"],
328 |             selected=(row["scope"].id == selected_id),
329 |             disclosure_reason=_reason(
330 |                 selected=(row["scope"].id == selected_id),
331 |                 eligible=row["eligible"],
332 |                 statistical_pass=row["statistical_pass"],
333 |                 min_population_pass=row["min_population_pass"],
334 |                 report_fraction_pass=row["report_fraction_pass"],
335 |                 qualifying=row["qualifying"],
336 |                 threshold=row["threshold"],
337 |                 n=row["scope"].population,
338 |                 min_pop=config.min_scope_population,
339 |                 fraction=row["fraction"],
340 |                 max_fraction=config.max_report_fraction,
341 |                 stability_note=stability_notes.get(row["scope"].id),
342 |             ),
343 |         )
344 |         for row in gate_rows
345 |     )
346 | 
347 |     return DisclosureResult(evaluations=evaluations, disclosed_scope_id=selected_id)

microcluster/engine.py

 1 | """Thin wiring between the engines.
 2 | 
 3 | Order is fixed (rule 10): intake caps first, then detection, then -- only
 4 | if detection fired -- disclosure. The disclosure engine receives the
 5 | detection engine's aggregate per-scope table, never the reports.
 6 | """
 7 | 
 8 | from __future__ import annotations
 9 | 
10 | from dataclasses import dataclass, field
11 | 
12 | from datetime import datetime
13 | 
14 | from . import detection, disclosure, intake
15 | from .config import (
16 |     DEFAULT_DETECTION_CONFIG,
17 |     DEFAULT_DISCLOSURE_CONFIG,
18 |     DEFAULT_INTAKE_CONFIG,
19 |     DetectionConfig,
20 |     DisclosureConfig,
21 |     IntakeConfig,
22 | )
23 | from .detection import DetectionResult, HysteresisState
24 | from .disclosure import DisclosureResult
25 | from .intake import IntakeResult, RejectedReport
26 | from .models import Report, ScopeRegistry
27 | 
28 | __all__ = ["AnalysisResult", "analyze"]
29 | 
30 | 
31 | @dataclass(frozen=True)
32 | class AnalysisResult:
33 |     detection: DetectionResult
34 |     disclosure: DisclosureResult
35 |     intake: IntakeResult | None = field(default=None)
36 | 
37 |     @property
38 |     def disclosed_scope_id(self) -> str | None:
39 |         return self.disclosure.disclosed_scope_id
40 | 
41 |     @property
42 |     def intake_rejected(self) -> tuple[RejectedReport, ...]:
43 |         return () if self.intake is None else self.intake.rejected
44 | 
45 | 
46 | def analyze(
47 |     reports: list[Report],
48 |     registry: ScopeRegistry,
49 |     *,
50 |     now: datetime | None = None,
51 |     detection_config: DetectionConfig = DEFAULT_DETECTION_CONFIG,
52 |     disclosure_config: DisclosureConfig = DEFAULT_DISCLOSURE_CONFIG,
53 |     intake_config: IntakeConfig | None = DEFAULT_INTAKE_CONFIG,
54 |     hysteresis_state: HysteresisState | None = None,
55 |     prior_disclosed_scope_id: str | None = None,
56 | ) -> AnalysisResult:
57 |     """Run intake caps, then detection, then -- if it fired -- disclosure.
58 | 
59 |     ``intake_config`` applies the per-session submission caps (rule 11)
60 |     before detection sees anything; only ``accepted`` reports go forward,
61 |     and any rejections are on ``result.intake``. Reports with no
62 |     ``session_id`` are never rate-limited, so a stream of anonymous
63 |     simulator reports is unaffected. Pass ``intake_config=None`` to skip
64 |     the caps entirely (``result.intake`` is then ``None``).
65 | 
66 |     ``hysteresis_state`` and ``prior_disclosed_scope_id`` thread state from
67 |     the PREVIOUS call in a sequence (e.g. yesterday's ``AnalysisResult``);
68 |     omit both for a one-off analysis. The next hysteresis state to pass
69 |     into the following call is on ``result.detection.hysteresis_state``,
70 |     and the scope to pass as next call's ``prior_disclosed_scope_id`` is
71 |     ``result.disclosed_scope_id``.
72 |     """
73 |     intake_result: IntakeResult | None = None
74 |     if intake_config is not None:
75 |         intake_result = intake.enforce_submission_caps(reports, config=intake_config)
76 |         reports = list(intake_result.accepted)
77 | 
78 |     det = detection.evaluate(
79 |         reports, registry, now=now, config=detection_config, hysteresis_state=hysteresis_state
80 |     )
81 | 
82 |     if not det.fired:
83 |         # Nothing detected -> the disclosure engine does not run at all.
84 |         return AnalysisResult(
85 |             det,
86 |             DisclosureResult(evaluations=(), disclosed_scope_id=None),
87 |             intake_result,
88 |         )
89 | 
90 |     dis = disclosure.evaluate(
91 |         registry,
92 |         det.qualifying_scope_counts,
93 |         config=disclosure_config,
94 |         prior_disclosed_scope_id=prior_disclosed_scope_id,
95 |     )
96 |     return AnalysisResult(det, dis, intake_result)

microcluster/intake.py

  1 | """Intake submission caps (rule 11).
  2 | 
  3 | The system is anonymous by design, so it cannot authenticate a reporter.
  4 | The only handle it has on a submission is an opaque, ephemeral
  5 | ``session_id`` the client sends with each report. This module caps how
  6 | many reports one session_id may file. That is the whole mitigation:
  7 | 
  8 |   * RATE LIMITING, NOT AUTHENTICATION. A report with ``session_id is
  9 |     None`` has no key to rate-limit and is always accepted. The simulator
 10 |     generates such reports (it is not modelling an intake client), so the
 11 |     normal pipeline is unaffected by this module.
 12 |   * A per-session cap is trivially defeated by using more sessions. An
 13 |     attacker who wants K fabricated reports past the cap needs
 14 |     ``ceil(K / session_submission_cap)`` sessions -- the same K reports,
 15 |     from more sources. ``adversarial.injection`` measures this directly.
 16 | 
 17 | Enforcement is a pure function over a batch of reports plus an
 18 | ``IntakeConfig``. It is wired into ``engine.analyze`` as the first step,
 19 | so a rejection is a normal engine outcome (``AnalysisResult.intake``),
 20 | never a silent drop.
 21 | """
 22 | 
 23 | from __future__ import annotations
 24 | 
 25 | from dataclasses import dataclass, field
 26 | 
 27 | from .config import DEFAULT_INTAKE_CONFIG, IntakeConfig
 28 | from .models import Report
 29 | 
 30 | __all__ = ["RejectedReport", "IntakeResult", "enforce_submission_caps"]
 31 | 
 32 | 
 33 | @dataclass(frozen=True)
 34 | class RejectedReport:
 35 |     """A report intake refused, with the reason. The report is kept so a
 36 |     caller can surface or log it -- it just never reaches detection."""
 37 | 
 38 |     report: Report
 39 |     reason: str
 40 | 
 41 | 
 42 | @dataclass(frozen=True)
 43 | class IntakeResult:
 44 |     accepted: tuple[Report, ...]
 45 |     rejected: tuple[RejectedReport, ...] = field(default=())
 46 | 
 47 |     @property
 48 |     def n_rejected(self) -> int:
 49 |         return len(self.rejected)
 50 | 
 51 | 
 52 | def enforce_submission_caps(
 53 |     reports: list[Report],
 54 |     *,
 55 |     config: IntakeConfig = DEFAULT_INTAKE_CONFIG,
 56 | ) -> IntakeResult:
 57 |     """Apply the per-session and per-session-per-day submission caps.
 58 | 
 59 |     Reports are considered in ``submitted_at`` order (stable for ties), so
 60 |     which reports of an over-cap session get through is deterministic: the
 61 |     earliest ones, up to the cap. A report is accepted only if, counting
 62 |     it, that session_id is still within BOTH:
 63 | 
 64 |       * ``session_submission_cap`` reports total (the session's lifetime,
 65 |         as far as this batch shows), and
 66 |       * ``daily_submission_cap`` reports on that report's calendar date.
 67 | 
 68 |     ``session_id is None`` -> always accepted (nothing to key on).
 69 |     """
 70 |     ordered = sorted(
 71 |         enumerate(reports), key=lambda pair: (pair[1].submitted_at, pair[0])
 72 |     )
 73 | 
 74 |     session_total: dict[str, int] = {}
 75 |     session_day: dict[tuple[str, object], int] = {}
 76 |     verdicts: dict[int, RejectedReport | None] = {}
 77 | 
 78 |     for original_index, report in ordered:
 79 |         sid = report.session_id
 80 |         if sid is None:
 81 |             verdicts[original_index] = None
 82 |             continue
 83 | 
 84 |         day_key = (sid, report.submitted_at.date())
 85 |         would_be_total = session_total.get(sid, 0) + 1
 86 |         would_be_day = session_day.get(day_key, 0) + 1
 87 | 
 88 |         if would_be_total > config.session_submission_cap:
 89 |             verdicts[original_index] = RejectedReport(
 90 |                 report,
 91 |                 f"session submission cap reached "
 92 |                 f"({config.session_submission_cap} per session)",
 93 |             )
 94 |         elif would_be_day > config.daily_submission_cap:
 95 |             verdicts[original_index] = RejectedReport(
 96 |                 report,
 97 |                 f"daily submission cap reached "
 98 |                 f"({config.daily_submission_cap} per session per day)",
 99 |             )
100 |         else:
101 |             session_total[sid] = would_be_total
102 |             session_day[day_key] = would_be_day
103 |             verdicts[original_index] = None
104 | 
105 |     accepted: list[Report] = []
106 |     rejected: list[RejectedReport] = []
107 |     for i, report in enumerate(reports):  # original order preserved
108 |         v = verdicts[i]
109 |         if v is None:
110 |             accepted.append(report)
111 |         else:
112 |             rejected.append(v)
113 | 
114 |     return IntakeResult(accepted=tuple(accepted), rejected=tuple(rejected))

microcluster/models.py

  1 | """Data model: the fixed checklist, scopes, and reports.
  2 | 
  3 | There is no free text anywhere in this module and no medical concept
  4 | finer than a *category* (rule 1, rule 2). A ``Report`` records a specific
  5 | symptom only for the reporter's own clarity; nothing in the engines ever
  6 | reads ``Report.symptom`` for matching.
  7 | """
  8 | 
  9 | from __future__ import annotations
 10 | 
 11 | from dataclasses import dataclass, field
 12 | from datetime import datetime
 13 | from enum import Enum, IntEnum
 14 | 
 15 | 
 16 | class Category(str, Enum):
 17 |     """The four fixed checklist categories. Clustering happens here and
 18 |     nowhere finer."""
 19 | 
 20 |     RESPIRATORY = "Respiratory"
 21 |     GASTROINTESTINAL = "Gastrointestinal"
 22 |     GENERAL = "General"
 23 |     OTHER = "Other"
 24 | 
 25 | 
 26 | # The fixed checklist: 3-5 named symptoms per category. The symptom text is
 27 | # a closed vocabulary chosen at design time -- it is NOT free text, and it
 28 | # is never used for matching (rule 2). It exists so a reporter can pick the
 29 | # thing that matches what they feel.
 30 | CHECKLIST: dict[Category, tuple[str, ...]] = {
 31 |     Category.RESPIRATORY: (
 32 |         "cough",
 33 |         "sore throat",
 34 |         "shortness of breath",
 35 |         "runny or blocked nose",
 36 |         "fever with cough",
 37 |     ),
 38 |     Category.GASTROINTESTINAL: (
 39 |         "nausea",
 40 |         "vomiting",
 41 |         "diarrhea",
 42 |         "stomach cramps",
 43 |     ),
 44 |     Category.GENERAL: (
 45 |         "fever",
 46 |         "fatigue",
 47 |         "body aches",
 48 |         "headache",
 49 |         "chills",
 50 |     ),
 51 |     Category.OTHER: (
 52 |         "skin rash",
 53 |         "dizziness",
 54 |         "loss of taste or smell",
 55 |     ),
 56 | }
 57 | 
 58 | 
 59 | class Onset(str, Enum):
 60 |     """Coarse onset buckets. Deliberately imprecise."""
 61 | 
 62 |     TODAY = "today"
 63 |     ONE_TO_TWO_DAYS = "1-2 days"
 64 |     THREE_TO_SEVEN_DAYS = "3-7 days"
 65 |     ONE_WEEK_PLUS = "1+ week"
 66 | 
 67 | 
 68 | class ScopeLevel(IntEnum):
 69 |     """Hierarchy depth. Larger value == finer scope. Used to pick the
 70 |     'finest' scope in rule 9 -- there is no hardcoded tier list, only this
 71 |     ordering over whatever scopes exist."""
 72 | 
 73 |     CAMPUS = 1
 74 |     BUILDING = 2
 75 |     FLOOR = 3
 76 |     SUITE = 4
 77 | 
 78 | 
 79 | @dataclass(frozen=True)
 80 | class Scope:
 81 |     """One node in the location hierarchy.
 82 | 
 83 |     ``population`` is DECLARED at group creation (rule 7). It is never the
 84 |     number of people who reported. Every gate in the disclosure engine
 85 |     uses this number.
 86 |     """
 87 | 
 88 |     id: str
 89 |     label: str
 90 |     level: ScopeLevel
 91 |     population: int
 92 |     parent_id: str | None = None
 93 | 
 94 |     def __post_init__(self) -> None:
 95 |         if self.population <= 0:
 96 |             raise ValueError(f"scope {self.id!r}: declared population must be positive")
 97 |         if self.level == ScopeLevel.CAMPUS and self.parent_id is not None:
 98 |             raise ValueError(f"scope {self.id!r}: a CAMPUS scope has no parent")
 99 |         if self.level != ScopeLevel.CAMPUS and self.parent_id is None:
100 |             raise ValueError(f"scope {self.id!r}: non-CAMPUS scope needs a parent_id")
101 | 
102 | 
103 | @dataclass(frozen=True)
104 | class ScopeRegistry:
105 |     """The declared set of scopes. Built once, at group creation."""
106 | 
107 |     scopes: dict[str, Scope]
108 | 
109 |     def __post_init__(self) -> None:
110 |         for scope_id, scope in self.scopes.items():
111 |             if scope.id != scope_id:
112 |                 raise ValueError(f"registry key {scope_id!r} != scope id {scope.id!r}")
113 |             if scope.parent_id is not None and scope.parent_id not in self.scopes:
114 |                 raise ValueError(f"scope {scope_id!r}: unknown parent {scope.parent_id!r}")
115 | 
116 |     def get(self, scope_id: str) -> Scope:
117 |         try:
118 |             return self.scopes[scope_id]
119 |         except KeyError:
120 |             raise KeyError(f"unknown scope id: {scope_id!r}") from None
121 | 
122 |     def chain_to_root(self, scope_id: str) -> list[Scope]:
123 |         """``scope_id`` itself, then each ancestor, up to the campus root."""
124 |         out: list[Scope] = []
125 |         current: str | None = scope_id
126 |         seen: set[str] = set()
127 |         while current is not None:
128 |             if current in seen:
129 |                 raise ValueError(f"scope cycle detected at {current!r}")
130 |             seen.add(current)
131 |             scope = self.get(current)
132 |             out.append(scope)
133 |             current = scope.parent_id
134 |         return out
135 | 
136 |     def contains(self, ancestor_id: str, scope_id: str) -> bool:
137 |         """True if ``scope_id`` is ``ancestor_id`` or sits below it."""
138 |         return any(s.id == ancestor_id for s in self.chain_to_root(scope_id))
139 | 
140 |     def roots(self) -> list[Scope]:
141 |         return [s for s in self.scopes.values() if s.parent_id is None]
142 | 
143 |     def campus_population(self) -> int:
144 |         """Total declared population: the sum of the root scopes'
145 |         declared populations. Used as the comparison denominator by both
146 |         detectors."""
147 |         roots = self.roots()
148 |         if not roots:
149 |             raise ValueError("registry has no root (CAMPUS) scope")
150 |         return sum(s.population for s in roots)
151 | 
152 | 
153 | @dataclass(frozen=True)
154 | class Report:
155 |     """One anonymous submission from the fixed checklist.
156 | 
157 |     ``session_id`` is an opaque, ephemeral token used only for the
158 |     intake-time submission caps (rule 11). The engines never read it.
159 |     """
160 | 
161 |     category: Category
162 |     symptom: str
163 |     onset: Onset
164 |     location_id: str
165 |     submitted_at: datetime
166 |     session_id: str | None = field(default=None)
167 | 
168 |     def __post_init__(self) -> None:
169 |         if not isinstance(self.category, Category):
170 |             raise ValueError(f"category must be a Category, got {self.category!r}")
171 |         if not isinstance(self.onset, Onset):
172 |             raise ValueError(f"onset must be an Onset, got {self.onset!r}")
173 |         if self.symptom not in CHECKLIST[self.category]:
174 |             raise ValueError(
175 |                 f"symptom {self.symptom!r} is not in the fixed checklist for "
176 |                 f"category {self.category.value!r}"
177 |             )

simulation/__init__.py

 1 | """Headless outbreak simulator.
 2 | 
 3 | Simulates an illness spreading suite-by-suite through a small shared
 4 | building, generates anonymous symptom reports from it, and feeds them to
 5 | ``microcluster``'s existing, unmodified detection engine. No rendering, no
 6 | API, no frontend.
 7 | 
 8 | It only ever *reads* from ``microcluster`` (``models``, ``config``,
 9 | ``engine``) and never modifies it. See ``simulation.pipeline`` for the
10 | end-to-end wiring, and CLAUDE.md's "ground-truth firewall" for what is and
11 | is not allowed to cross from this package into the production engines.
12 | """
