simulation/agent.py

 1 | """The Agent: one simulated person."""
 2 | 
 3 | from __future__ import annotations
 4 | 
 5 | from dataclasses import dataclass
 6 | 
 7 | from .infection import InfectionState
 8 | 
 9 | 
10 | @dataclass
11 | class Agent:
12 |     """One simulated person.
13 | 
14 |     An agent belongs to exactly one suite. Every ``Report`` it produces
15 |     (see ``simulation.reporting``) uses ``suite_id`` as the
16 |     ``location_id`` -- always, and only that (detection.evaluate rejects
17 |     mixed-level input).
18 | 
19 |     ``incubation_days`` / ``symptomatic_days`` are drawn when the agent is
20 |     infected and are ``None`` before then.
21 |     """
22 | 
23 |     id: str
24 |     suite_id: str
25 |     # Personal probability of filing a report while SYMPTOMATIC. Not
26 |     # everyone reports; assigned once at population creation.
27 |     reporting_probability: float
28 |     state: InfectionState = InfectionState.SUSCEPTIBLE
29 |     # Day on which ``state`` last changed (0 = start of the run).
30 |     state_changed_day: int = 0
31 |     incubation_days: int | None = None
32 |     symptomatic_days: int | None = None
33 |     # Whether this agent has already filed its (at most one) report for
34 |     # the current illness episode. Reports are anonymous, so this flag
35 |     # lives only here -- it never appears on a Report.
36 |     has_reported: bool = False
37 | 
38 |     def __post_init__(self) -> None:
39 |         if not 0.0 <= self.reporting_probability <= 1.0:
40 |             raise ValueError(
41 |                 f"agent {self.id}: reporting_probability "
42 |                 f"{self.reporting_probability!r} is outside [0, 1]"
43 |             )
44 | 
45 | 
46 | @dataclass(frozen=True)
47 | class AgentSnapshot:
48 |     """Immutable view of one agent at the end of a simulated day."""
49 | 
50 |     id: str
51 |     suite_id: str
52 |     state: InfectionState
53 |     state_changed_day: int

simulation/benchmark.py

  1 | """Learned detector vs the hand-authored threshold rules.
  2 | 
  3 | Still headless -- no plotting. Builds a labelled dataset from simulated
  4 | runs, trains a small classifier on it, and scores that classifier against
  5 | the authored rules in ``microcluster`` on HELD-OUT runs.
  6 | 
  7 |     python -m simulation.benchmark
  8 | 
  9 | ---------------------------------------------------------------------
 10 | WHAT "ONE ROW PER (day, scope)" MEANS HERE
 11 | ---------------------------------------------------------------------
 12 | 
 13 | ``microcluster.detection`` operates at exactly one scope: the campus, the
 14 | level its two detectors compare against (a location versus its campus
 15 | peers; total volume versus the campus background expectation). It emits
 16 | its three plain findings once per evaluation. So the dataset is one row
 17 | per (run, day): the features are that day's three findings computed on the
 18 | reports accumulated so far, and the label is ground truth for that day.
 19 | This is the unit the authored rules also produce a decision at, which is
 20 | what makes the head-to-head comparison well posed.
 21 | 
 22 | Features (exactly the three findings ``microcluster.detection`` reports,
 23 | rule 6 -- no composite, nothing finer than a category):
 24 | 
 25 |   * ``relative_excess_ratio``   -- the hotspot location's smoothed per-capita
 26 |                                    rate over its peers'
 27 |   * ``count_within_window``     -- qualifying reports in the 72h window
 28 |   * ``count_sharing_category``  -- how many of those share the dominant category
 29 | 
 30 | Label (from ground truth, never shown to the engines): 1 iff on that day
 31 | the outbreak is BOTH established (cumulative ever-infected >=
 32 | ``ESTABLISHED_MIN_INFECTIONS``) AND still active (at least one agent
 33 | INCUBATING or SYMPTOMATIC). This is a CHOSEN definition of "an outbreak
 34 | the system ought to be flagging right now", in the same spirit as every
 35 | threshold in ``config.py``; it is not a measured quantity.
 36 | 
 37 | ---------------------------------------------------------------------
 38 | METHODOLOGY
 39 | ---------------------------------------------------------------------
 40 | 
 41 | * SPLIT BY RUN, never by day. Adjacent days from one outbreak are highly
 42 |   correlated; letting them straddle the split would leak the test set into
 43 |   training and flatter the model.
 44 | 
 45 | * CROSS-REGIME EVALUATION. A model trained on this simulator learns this
 46 |   simulator. The benchmark therefore also trains on one transmission
 47 |   regime (the shipped ``SUITE_TRANSMISSION_PROBABILITY``) and evaluates on
 48 |   runs generated with a different one (``BENCHMARK_TEST_SUITE_TRANSMISSIONS``
 49 |   -- a slower and a faster epidemic). If the model only beats the rules on
 50 |   its training distribution, ``main`` says so in plain language. A clean
 51 |   negative result is reported, not hidden.
 52 | 
 53 | * THE MODEL NEVER REPLACES THE RULES. Both stay in the codebase. This
 54 |   module scores them side by side and nothing more; the disclosure path
 55 |   still runs on the authored rules alone.
 56 | """
 57 | 
 58 | from __future__ import annotations
 59 | 
 60 | import random
 61 | import sys
 62 | from collections import defaultdict
 63 | from dataclasses import dataclass
 64 | from datetime import timedelta
 65 | from pathlib import Path
 66 | from statistics import mean
 67 | from typing import Sequence
 68 | 
 69 | sys.path.insert(0, str(Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent))
 70 | 
 71 | import numpy as np  # noqa: E402
 72 | from sklearn.linear_model import LogisticRegression  # noqa: E402
 73 | from sklearn.tree import DecisionTreeClassifier  # noqa: E402
 74 | 
 75 | from microcluster.config import DEFAULT_DETECTION_CONFIG, DetectionConfig  # noqa: E402
 76 | from microcluster.detection import evaluate as detection_evaluate  # noqa: E402
 77 | from microcluster.models import Report  # noqa: E402
 78 | 
 79 | from simulation.config import (  # noqa: E402
 80 |     BENCHMARK_DEFAULT_SEEDS,
 81 |     BENCHMARK_SPLIT_SEED,
 82 |     BENCHMARK_TEST_RUN_FRACTION,
 83 |     BENCHMARK_TEST_SUITE_TRANSMISSIONS,
 84 |     BENCHMARK_TRAIN_SUITE_TRANSMISSION,
 85 |     BENCHMARK_TREE_MAX_DEPTH,
 86 |     DEFAULT_RUN_DAYS,
 87 |     DEFAULT_SIMULATION_CONFIG,
 88 |     ESTABLISHED_MIN_INFECTIONS,
 89 |     SimulationConfig,
 90 | )
 91 | from simulation.infection import InfectionState  # noqa: E402
 92 | from simulation.pipeline import simulate_and_report  # noqa: E402
 93 | 
 94 | __all__ = [
 95 |     "FEATURE_NAMES",
 96 |     "DayRow",
 97 |     "build_dataset",
 98 |     "split_by_run",
 99 |     "train_model",
100 |     "Score",
101 |     "score_rows",
102 |     "score_model",
103 |     "score_rules",
104 |     "benchmark_in_distribution",
105 |     "benchmark_cross_regime",
106 | ]
107 | 
108 | FEATURE_NAMES: tuple[str, ...] = (
109 |     "relative_excess_ratio",
110 |     "count_within_window",
111 |     "count_sharing_category",
112 | )
113 | 
114 | 
115 | # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
116 | # Dataset
117 | # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
118 | 
119 | 
120 | @dataclass(frozen=True)
121 | class DayRow:
122 |     """One (run, day): the authored detector's three findings, the authored
123 |     rule's own verdict, and the ground-truth label."""
124 | 
125 |     seed: int
126 |     day: int
127 |     relative_excess_ratio: float
128 |     count_within_window: int
129 |     count_sharing_category: int
130 |     label: int  # 1 iff established AND active on this day (ground truth)
131 |     rule_fired: bool  # microcluster.detection raw single-instant verdict
132 | 
133 | 
134 | def _cumulative_infected(agents) -> int:
135 |     return sum(1 for a in agents if a.state is not InfectionState.SUSCEPTIBLE)
136 | 
137 | 
138 | def _active_infected(agents) -> int:
139 |     return sum(
140 |         1
141 |         for a in agents
142 |         if a.state in (InfectionState.INCUBATING, InfectionState.SYMPTOMATIC)
143 |     )
144 | 
145 | 
146 | def _ground_truth_label(agents, established_min_infections: int) -> int:
147 |     established = _cumulative_infected(agents) >= established_min_infections
148 |     active = _active_infected(agents) > 0
149 |     return int(established and active)
150 | 
151 | 
152 | def build_dataset(
153 |     seeds: Sequence[int],
154 |     *,
155 |     days: int = DEFAULT_RUN_DAYS,
156 |     config: SimulationConfig = DEFAULT_SIMULATION_CONFIG,
157 |     detection_config: DetectionConfig = DEFAULT_DETECTION_CONFIG,
158 |     established_min_infections: int = ESTABLISHED_MIN_INFECTIONS,
159 | ) -> list[DayRow]:
160 |     """Simulate each seed once and emit one :class:`DayRow` per simulated
161 |     day (day 0 .. ``days``). Ground truth is read straight from the
162 |     simulator's history; only ``Report`` objects and the registry are ever
163 |     passed to ``microcluster.detection`` (the ground-truth firewall)."""
164 |     rows: list[DayRow] = []
165 |     for seed in seeds:
166 |         simulated = simulate_and_report(seed=seed, days=days, config=config)
167 |         sim = simulated.simulation
168 |         accumulated: list[Report] = []
169 |         for day, day_reports in enumerate(simulated.daily_reports):
170 |             accumulated.extend(day_reports)
171 |             now = config.simulation_start + timedelta(days=day)
172 |             result = detection_evaluate(
173 |                 accumulated, sim.registry, now=now, config=detection_config
174 |             )
175 |             snapshot = sim.history[day]
176 |             rows.append(
177 |                 DayRow(
178 |                     seed=seed,
179 |                     day=day,
180 |                     relative_excess_ratio=result.findings.relative_excess_ratio,
181 |                     count_within_window=result.findings.count_within_window,
182 |                     count_sharing_category=result.findings.count_sharing_category,
183 |                     label=_ground_truth_label(snapshot.agents, established_min_infections),
184 |                     rule_fired=result.raw_fired,
185 |                 )
186 |             )
187 |     return rows
188 | 
189 | 
190 | def split_by_run(
191 |     rows: Sequence[DayRow],
192 |     *,
193 |     test_fraction: float = BENCHMARK_TEST_RUN_FRACTION,
194 |     split_seed: int = BENCHMARK_SPLIT_SEED,
195 | ) -> tuple[list[DayRow], list[DayRow]]:
196 |     """Partition ``rows`` into (train, test) by SEED. No seed appears in
197 |     both halves."""
198 |     seeds = sorted({r.seed for r in rows})
199 |     rng = random.Random(split_seed)
200 |     rng.shuffle(seeds)
201 |     n_test = max(1, round(len(seeds) * test_fraction))
202 |     test_seeds = set(seeds[:n_test])
203 |     train = [r for r in rows if r.seed not in test_seeds]
204 |     test = [r for r in rows if r.seed in test_seeds]
205 |     return train, test
206 | 
207 | 
208 | # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
209 | # Model
210 | # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
211 | 
212 | 
213 | def _feature_matrix(rows: Sequence[DayRow]) -> np.ndarray:
214 |     return np.array(
215 |         [
216 |             [
217 |                 r.relative_excess_ratio,
218 |                 r.count_within_window,
219 |                 r.count_sharing_category,
220 |             ]
221 |             for r in rows
222 |         ],
223 |         dtype=float,
224 |     )
225 | 
226 | 
227 | def _labels(rows: Sequence[DayRow]) -> np.ndarray:
228 |     return np.array([r.label for r in rows], dtype=int)
229 | 
230 | 
231 | def train_model(
232 |     train_rows: Sequence[DayRow],
233 |     *,
234 |     kind: str = "logistic",
235 |     tree_max_depth: int = BENCHMARK_TREE_MAX_DEPTH,
236 | ):
237 |     """Fit a small classifier on the three findings. ``kind`` is
238 |     ``"logistic"`` (default) or ``"tree"`` -- both deliberately low
239 |     capacity, so a win is a real signal and not memorisation."""
240 |     y = _labels(train_rows)
241 |     if len(np.unique(y)) < 2:
242 |         raise ValueError(
243 |             "training set has a single class; cannot fit a classifier "
244 |             "(need both established-and-active and not days)"
245 |         )
246 |     X = _feature_matrix(train_rows)
247 |     if kind == "tree":
248 |         model = DecisionTreeClassifier(max_depth=tree_max_depth, random_state=0)
249 |     elif kind == "logistic":
250 |         model = LogisticRegression(max_iter=1000, class_weight="balanced")
251 |     else:
252 |         raise ValueError(f"unknown model kind {kind!r}")
253 |     model.fit(X, y)
254 |     return model
255 | 
256 | 
257 | # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
258 | # Scoring
259 | # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
260 | 
261 | 
262 | @dataclass(frozen=True)
263 | class Score:
264 |     """Row-level precision/recall plus a per-run detection-delay summary."""
265 | 
266 |     label: str  # "model (logistic)", "authored rules", ...
267 |     precision: float
268 |     recall: float
269 |     n_predicted_positive: int
270 |     n_actual_positive: int
271 |     n_runs_with_outbreak: int
272 |     n_runs_detected: int
273 |     mean_detection_delay: float | None  # days, over detected runs only
274 |     never_detected_runs: int
275 | 
276 | 
277 | def score_rows(
278 |     rows: Sequence[DayRow], predictions: Sequence[int], *, label: str
279 | ) -> Score:
280 |     """Score a 0/1 prediction per row. Detection delay per run: first
281 |     positive prediction on or after the run's ground-truth onset day, minus
282 |     that onset day; runs never predicted positive after onset count as
283 |     'never detected'."""
284 |     preds = list(predictions)
285 |     tp = sum(1 for r, p in zip(rows, preds) if p and r.label)
286 |     fp = sum(1 for r, p in zip(rows, preds) if p and not r.label)
287 |     fn = sum(1 for r, p in zip(rows, preds) if not p and r.label)
288 |     precision = tp / (tp + fp) if (tp + fp) else 0.0
289 |     recall = tp / (tp + fn) if (tp + fn) else 0.0
290 | 
291 |     by_run: dict[int, list[tuple[int, int, int]]] = defaultdict(list)
292 |     for r, p in zip(rows, preds):
293 |         by_run[r.seed].append((r.day, r.label, p))
294 | 
295 |     delays: list[int] = []
296 |     runs_with_outbreak = 0
297 |     never = 0
298 |     for items in by_run.values():
299 |         items.sort()
300 |         onset_days = [d for d, lbl, _ in items if lbl]
301 |         if not onset_days:
302 |             continue
303 |         runs_with_outbreak += 1
304 |         onset = onset_days[0]
305 |         fired_after = [d for d, _, p in items if p and d >= onset]
306 |         if fired_after:
307 |             delays.append(fired_after[0] - onset)
308 |         else:
309 |             never += 1
310 | 
311 |     return Score(
312 |         label=label,
313 |         precision=precision,
314 |         recall=recall,
315 |         n_predicted_positive=tp + fp,
316 |         n_actual_positive=tp + fn,
317 |         n_runs_with_outbreak=runs_with_outbreak,
318 |         n_runs_detected=runs_with_outbreak - never,
319 |         mean_detection_delay=mean(delays) if delays else None,
320 |         never_detected_runs=never,
321 |     )
322 | 
323 | 
324 | def score_model(model, test_rows: Sequence[DayRow], *, label: str) -> Score:
325 |     preds = model.predict(_feature_matrix(test_rows)).astype(int).tolist()
326 |     return score_rows(test_rows, preds, label=label)
327 | 
328 | 
329 | def score_rules(test_rows: Sequence[DayRow], *, label: str = "authored rules") -> Score:
330 |     return score_rows(
331 |         test_rows, [int(r.rule_fired) for r in test_rows], label=label
332 |     )
333 | 
334 | 
335 | # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
336 | # The two benchmarks
337 | # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
338 | 
339 | 
340 | @dataclass(frozen=True)
341 | class BenchmarkResult:
342 |     heading: str
343 |     n_train_rows: int
344 |     n_test_rows: int
345 |     scores: tuple[Score, ...]  # rules first, then each model
346 | 
347 | 
348 | def benchmark_in_distribution(
349 |     seeds: Sequence[int],
350 |     *,
351 |     days: int = DEFAULT_RUN_DAYS,
352 |     config: SimulationConfig = DEFAULT_SIMULATION_CONFIG,
353 | ) -> BenchmarkResult:
354 |     rows = build_dataset(seeds, days=days, config=config)
355 |     train, test = split_by_run(rows)
356 |     logistic = train_model(train, kind="logistic")
357 |     tree = train_model(train, kind="tree")
358 |     return BenchmarkResult(
359 |         heading="IN DISTRIBUTION (train and test are the same transmission regime)",
360 |         n_train_rows=len(train),
361 |         n_test_rows=len(test),
362 |         scores=(
363 |             score_rules(test),
364 |             score_model(logistic, test, label="model (logistic regression)"),
365 |             score_model(tree, test, label=f"model (tree, depth {BENCHMARK_TREE_MAX_DEPTH})"),
366 |         ),
367 |     )
368 | 
369 | 
370 | def benchmark_cross_regime(
371 |     train_seeds: Sequence[int],
372 |     test_seeds: Sequence[int],
373 |     *,
374 |     days: int = DEFAULT_RUN_DAYS,
375 |     train_suite_transmission: float = BENCHMARK_TRAIN_SUITE_TRANSMISSION,
376 |     test_suite_transmissions: Sequence[float] = BENCHMARK_TEST_SUITE_TRANSMISSIONS,
377 | ) -> list[BenchmarkResult]:
378 |     """Train once on ``train_suite_transmission``; evaluate on held-out
379 |     seeds simulated at each of ``test_suite_transmissions``."""
380 |     train_config = SimulationConfig(suite_transmission_probability=train_suite_transmission)
381 |     train_rows = build_dataset(train_seeds, days=days, config=train_config)
382 |     logistic = train_model(train_rows, kind="logistic")
383 |     tree = train_model(train_rows, kind="tree")
384 | 
385 |     results: list[BenchmarkResult] = []
386 |     for tp in test_suite_transmissions:
387 |         test_config = SimulationConfig(suite_transmission_probability=tp)
388 |         test_rows = build_dataset(test_seeds, days=days, config=test_config)
389 |         results.append(
390 |             BenchmarkResult(
391 |                 heading=(
392 |                     f"CROSS REGIME (trained at suite p={train_suite_transmission:g}, "
393 |                     f"tested at suite p={tp:g})"
394 |                 ),
395 |                 n_train_rows=len(train_rows),
396 |                 n_test_rows=len(test_rows),
397 |                 scores=(
398 |                     score_rules(test_rows),
399 |                     score_model(logistic, test_rows, label="model (logistic regression)"),
400 |                     score_model(
401 |                         tree, test_rows, label=f"model (tree, depth {BENCHMARK_TREE_MAX_DEPTH})"
402 |                     ),
403 |                 ),
404 |             )
405 |         )
406 |     return results
407 | 
408 | 
409 | # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
410 | # Reporting
411 | # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
412 | 
413 | 
414 | def _fmt(value: float | None, decimals: int = 2) -> str:
415 |     return "n/a" if value is None else f"{value:.{decimals}f}"
416 | 
417 | 
418 | def _print_result(result: BenchmarkResult) -> None:
419 |     print(f"--- {result.heading} ---")
420 |     print(f"    {result.n_train_rows} train rows, {result.n_test_rows} test rows")
421 |     cols = [
422 |         ("detector", 30),
423 |         ("precision", 10),
424 |         ("recall", 8),
425 |         ("delay", 7),
426 |         ("detected", 12),
427 |     ]
428 |     header = "  ".join(f"{name:>{w}}" if name != "detector" else f"{name:<{w}}" for name, w in cols)
429 |     print(header)
430 |     print("-" * len(header))
431 |     for s in result.scores:
432 |         detected = f"{s.n_runs_detected}/{s.n_runs_with_outbreak}"
433 |         row = (
434 |             f"{s.label:<30}",
435 |             f"{_fmt(s.precision):>10}",
436 |             f"{_fmt(s.recall):>8}",
437 |             f"{_fmt(s.mean_detection_delay, 1):>7}",
438 |             f"{detected:>12}",
439 |         )
440 |         print("  ".join(row))
441 |     print()
442 | 
443 | 
444 | def _tradeoff(model: Score, rules: Score) -> str:
445 |     """One plain line comparing a model score to the rules score."""
446 |     dp = model.precision - rules.precision
447 |     dr = model.recall - rules.recall
448 |     md, rd = model.mean_detection_delay, rules.mean_detection_delay
449 |     dd = None if (md is None or rd is None) else md - rd
450 |     return (
451 |         f"precision {dp:+.2f}, recall {dr:+.2f}, "
452 |         f"delay {'n/a' if dd is None else f'{dd:+.1f}d'}"
453 |     )
454 | 
455 | 
456 | def _verdict(in_dist: BenchmarkResult, cross: list[BenchmarkResult]) -> None:
457 |     print("=" * 78)
458 |     print("VERDICT  (logistic model vs authored rules, deltas are model minus rules)")
459 |     print("=" * 78)
460 |     print(f"  in distribution                         : {_tradeoff(in_dist.scores[1], in_dist.scores[0])}")
461 |     cross_deltas = []
462 |     for res in cross:
463 |         d = res.scores[1].precision - res.scores[0].precision, res.scores[1].recall - res.scores[0].recall
464 |         cross_deltas.append(d)
465 |         tail = res.heading.split("tested at ")[-1].rstrip(")")
466 |         print(f"  cross regime, {tail:26} : {_tradeoff(res.scores[1], res.scores[0])}")
467 |     print()
468 |     recall_gain_everywhere = all(dr > 0.02 for _, dr in cross_deltas) and (
469 |         in_dist.scores[1].recall - in_dist.scores[0].recall > 0.02
470 |     )
471 |     precision_cost = -min(
472 |         [in_dist.scores[1].precision - in_dist.scores[0].precision]
473 |         + [dp for dp, _ in cross_deltas]
474 |     )
475 |     if recall_gain_everywhere:
476 |         print("  The model consistently catches more real outbreak-days and fires about a")
477 |         print(f"  day sooner, IN AND OUT of its training regime, at a cost of up to")
478 |         print(f"  {precision_cost:.2f} in precision. The gain survives the transmission-probability")
479 |         print("  shift, so it is not purely memorised -- but it is small, it is bought with")
480 |         print("  false positives, and it is still a model of THIS simulator. The authored")
481 |         print("  rules remain the shipped detector; the model is kept only as this benchmark.")
482 |     else:
483 |         print("  The model's advantage does not hold up once the transmission probability")
484 |         print("  differs from training. Reported as a negative result: the classifier")
485 |         print("  mostly learned this simulator. The three authored thresholds are the")
486 |         print("  stronger, and the shipped, choice.")
487 |     print()
488 | 
489 | 
490 | def main() -> None:
491 |     n = BENCHMARK_DEFAULT_SEEDS
492 |     all_seeds = list(range(1, n + 1))
493 |     bar = "=" * 78
494 | 
495 |     print(bar)
496 |     print(f"LEARNED DETECTOR vs AUTHORED RULES   ({n} simulated runs, {DEFAULT_RUN_DAYS}-day)")
497 |     print("features: relative_excess_ratio, count_within_window, count_sharing_category")
498 |     print(f"label   : established (cumulative >= {ESTABLISHED_MIN_INFECTIONS}) AND active, from ground truth")
499 |     print("split   : by RUN, never by day")
500 |     print(bar)
501 |     print()
502 | 
503 |     in_dist = benchmark_in_distribution(all_seeds)
504 |     _print_result(in_dist)
505 | 
506 |     # Cross-regime: reuse the same split so train/test seeds never overlap.
507 |     train_seeds, test_seeds = split_by_run(
508 |         [DayRow(s, 0, 0.0, 0, 0, 0, False) for s in all_seeds]
509 |     )
510 |     train_ids = sorted({r.seed for r in train_seeds})
511 |     test_ids = sorted({r.seed for r in test_seeds})
512 |     cross = benchmark_cross_regime(train_ids, test_ids)
513 |     for res in cross:
514 |         _print_result(res)
515 | 
516 |     print("  delay = mean days from ground-truth outbreak onset to first positive call")
517 |     print("          (over runs that were detected at all); detected = runs called / runs")
518 |     print("          with a real outbreak.")
519 |     print()
520 |     _verdict(in_dist, cross)
521 | 
522 | 
523 | if __name__ == "__main__":
524 |     main()

simulation/config.py

  1 | """Tunable constants for the outbreak simulator.
  2 | 
  3 | EVERY value the simulation depends on lives here as a named constant with
  4 | a comment, exactly as in ``microcluster/config.py``. Nothing elsewhere in
  5 | the ``simulation`` package hard-codes a tunable number.
  6 | 
  7 | None of these figures is clinical or epidemiological. They are chosen to
  8 | produce a legible demonstration outbreak on a small population, not
  9 | measured from real data. Tune them before drawing any real conclusion.
 10 | 
 11 | PROVENANCE OF THESE NUMBERS
 12 | --------------------------------------------------------------------------
 13 | There is no real dataset behind this simulator, so there are no citations
 14 | to give for the specific values. Every transmission probability, reporting
 15 | rate, contact rate and state duration below was PICKED FOR DEMONSTRATION:
 16 | set by hand, checked by running the model, and kept at the figure that
 17 | produced a believable outbreak on a 150-person building inside a 30-day
 18 | window (see the joint-tuning note at ``SUITE_TRANSMISSION_PROBABILITY``).
 19 | 
 20 | Where the SHAPE of a modelling choice follows standard practice, that is
 21 | noted, and only the shape -- not any number -- is borrowed:
 22 | 
 23 | * compartmental progression susceptible -> incubating -> symptomatic ->
 24 |   recovered is the classic SIR / SEIR structure (Kermack & McKendrick,
 25 |   1927; and every agent-based descendant since).
 26 | * tiered per-contact transmission by shared space (suite / floor /
 27 |   building) is the ordinary agent-based-model treatment of contact
 28 |   structure.
 29 | 
 30 | The specific numbers are ours and illustrative only. Anything user-facing
 31 | says so in the same words (see ``api.app.DISCLAIMER`` and the on-page
 32 | limitations banner).
 33 | """
 34 | 
 35 | from __future__ import annotations
 36 | 
 37 | from dataclasses import dataclass
 38 | from datetime import datetime, timezone
 39 | 
 40 | from microcluster.config import BACKGROUND_RATE, DETECTION_WINDOW_HOURS
 41 | from microcluster.models import Category
 42 | 
 43 | # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
 44 | # Infection state machine: state durations (illustrative, NOT clinical)
 45 | # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
 46 | 
 47 | # On infection an agent spends this many days INCUBATING (already
 48 | # infectious, not yet reporting) before turning SYMPTOMATIC. Drawn
 49 | # uniformly per agent from this inclusive range. Kept short, together with
 50 | # SYMPTOMATIC_DAYS below, so a seeded suite's outbreak runs its course
 51 | # (and can burn out at a PARTIAL attack rate) inside the 30-day smoke
 52 | # window, rather than smouldering indefinitely -- see
 53 | # SUITE_TRANSMISSION_PROBABILITY below for how the two are tuned jointly.
 54 | INCUBATION_DAYS_MIN: int = 1
 55 | INCUBATION_DAYS_MAX: int = 2
 56 | 
 57 | # Once SYMPTOMATIC an agent stays symptomatic this many days before moving
 58 | # to RECOVERED. Drawn uniformly per agent from this inclusive range.
 59 | SYMPTOMATIC_DAYS_MIN: int = 1
 60 | SYMPTOMATIC_DAYS_MAX: int = 5
 61 | 
 62 | 
 63 | # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
 64 | # Contact model: tiered by scope, reached through the ScopeRegistry
 65 | # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
 66 | #
 67 | # Contact is not suite-only. On each daily step an infectious agent can
 68 | # transmit to a susceptible agent that shares its SUITE, its FLOOR (a
 69 | # different suite on the same floor), or its BUILDING (a different floor
 70 | # in the same building) -- at three different, decreasing per-contact
 71 | # probabilities. Campus-level contact is not modelled: two people who only
 72 | # share a campus are assumed not to meet. Which suite/floor/building an
 73 | # agent belongs to is read from the ScopeRegistry (chain_to_root), never
 74 | # hard-coded from id strings.
 75 | #
 76 | # Each probability compounds over however many infectious contacts an
 77 | # agent has at that tier:
 78 | #   P(infected today) = 1 - (1-p_suite)**k_suite
 79 | #                          * (1-p_floor)**k_floor
 80 | #                          * (1-p_building)**k_building
 81 | #
 82 | # All three, together with the state durations above, are chosen jointly
 83 | # and EMPIRICALLY (by running the model, not solved analytically) so that
 84 | # a single seeded suite typically reaches a PARTIAL, seed-varying attack
 85 | # rate -- roughly in the 40-70% range on most seeds, occasionally higher,
 86 | # occasionally fizzling out entirely from just the one index case -- rather
 87 | # than either dying out every time or deterministically infecting everyone.
 88 | # A small, fully-mixed suite is close to an all-or-nothing branching
 89 | # process: a real outbreak sometimes just fails to take off, and that is
 90 | # reported honestly (a low attack rate), not smoothed away. Floor and
 91 | # building transmission are lower than suite transmission but not
 92 | # negligible, so the outbreak reaches a second suite, and sometimes a
 93 | # second floor, well before it reaches everyone on the first floor.
 94 | 
 95 | # Probability that one infectious agent infects one susceptible SUITE-mate
 96 | # on a single day.
 97 | SUITE_TRANSMISSION_PROBABILITY: float = 0.008
 98 | 
 99 | # Probability that one infectious agent infects one susceptible agent in a
100 | # DIFFERENT suite on the SAME FLOOR, on a single day. Lower than the suite
101 | # rate: floor-mates share common areas but not a room.
102 | FLOOR_TRANSMISSION_PROBABILITY: float = 0.003
103 | 
104 | # Probability that one infectious agent infects one susceptible agent on a
105 | # DIFFERENT floor of the SAME BUILDING, on a single day. Lower still: the
106 | # only shared contact is building-wide common space.
107 | BUILDING_TRANSMISSION_PROBABILITY: float = 0.001
108 | 
109 | 
110 | # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
111 | # Reporting behaviour
112 | # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
113 | 
114 | # Each agent is assigned, once at population creation, a personal
115 | # probability of filing a symptom report while SYMPTOMATIC. Not everyone
116 | # reports. Drawn uniformly per agent from this inclusive range. No real
117 | # source: a spread picked for demo so the detector has a partial, noisy
118 | # signal to work with rather than a full census of the sick.
119 | REPORTING_PROBABILITY_MIN: float = 0.30
120 | REPORTING_PROBABILITY_MAX: float = 0.70
121 | 
122 | # The outbreak clusters at ONE category, chosen once per simulation (rule
123 | # 2: clustering is at category level only). The specific symptom text is
124 | # still drawn from the fixed CHECKLIST for realism, but -- as everywhere
125 | # else in this project -- it is never used for matching.
126 | OUTBREAK_CATEGORY: Category = Category.RESPIRATORY
127 | 
128 | # BACKGROUND NOISE. Agents who are NOT currently symptomatic from the
129 | # outbreak also file unrelated reports (a sore throat that is just a sore
130 | # throat, a stomach bug that has nothing to do with any of this), each day,
131 | # independently, at this rate, in a category OTHER than OUTBREAK_CATEGORY.
132 | #
133 | # This is what makes the detection problem real. Without background noise,
134 | # every single report in the stream is outbreak signal, the absolute
135 | # detector's BACKGROUND_RATE comparison is meaningless (there is no
136 | # background to compare against), and detection is trivial. With it, the
137 | # detector has to find a category-level cluster inside a stream that also
138 | # contains ordinary, everyday, unrelated complaints.
139 | #
140 | # Chosen to land in the neighbourhood of microcluster.config.BACKGROUND_RATE
141 | # (the illustrative fraction of a population expected to report per
142 | # DETECTION_WINDOW_HOURS window under ordinary conditions), spread evenly
143 | # across that window's days: BACKGROUND_RATE / (DETECTION_WINDOW_HOURS / 24).
144 | BACKGROUND_NOISE_DAILY_RATE: float = BACKGROUND_RATE / (DETECTION_WINDOW_HOURS / 24)
145 | 
146 | # Reference wall-clock instant for simulated day 0. Every Report's
147 | # ``submitted_at`` and every call to ``microcluster.engine.analyze`` is
148 | # timestamped relative to this, so the 72-hour detection window is
149 | # evaluated against SIMULATED time, never the real clock.
150 | SIMULATION_START: datetime = datetime(2026, 1, 1, tzinfo=timezone.utc)
151 | 
152 | 
153 | # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
154 | # Seeding and run length
155 | # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
156 | 
157 | # Agents infected at day 0 when a run starts.
158 | DEFAULT_SEED_INFECTIONS: int = 1
159 | 
160 | # Length of the smoke-test run, in days.
161 | DEFAULT_RUN_DAYS: int = 30
162 | 
163 | # Random seeds the smoke-test entry point iterates over.
164 | DEFAULT_SMOKE_SEEDS: tuple[int, ...] = (1, 2, 3, 4, 5)
165 | 
166 | 
167 | # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
168 | # Experiment runner (simulation.experiments): establishment and the sweep
169 | # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
170 | #
171 | # A single seeded index case is a stochastic branching process: a real
172 | # fraction of runs fail to take off at all, purely by chance, before the
173 | # detector or the disclosure policy ever get a say. An outbreak counts as
174 | # ESTABLISHED once its cumulative (ever-infected) case count reaches this
175 | # many -- below it, the run is a fade-out from the index case, not a real
176 | # event. This is a METHODOLOGICAL CHOICE, not a modelling fact: see
177 | # simulation/experiments.py's module docstring for why it matters and how
178 | # it is applied (report the fade-out rate on its own; condition every
179 | # other statistic on establishment; never condition the false-alarm rate
180 | # on it, since that is measured over the runs that did NOT establish).
181 | ESTABLISHED_MIN_INFECTIONS: int = 5
182 | 
183 | # Default number of random seeds the experiment runner sweeps over.
184 | DEFAULT_EXPERIMENT_SEEDS: int = 500
185 | 
186 | # The delay-versus-infections curve sweeps the DISCLOSURE gates, not the
187 | # detection threshold. An earlier attempt swept ABSOLUTE_EXCESS_RATIO and
188 | # found time-to-disclosure completely invariant to it: with detection
189 | # firing well before disclosure clears anyway, it is the disclosure gates
190 | # that set the timing. Detection config is held at defaults for all three
191 | # sweeps below.
192 | #
193 | # Every one of these gates ultimately compares an INTEGER qualifying-report
194 | # count (or a declared population) against a threshold, so each parameter
195 | # only changes behaviour when it crosses a discrete boundary -- the scope
196 | # populations are 25 / 75 / 150 (suite / floor / building = campus, since
197 | # the default structure has one building), and ceil(sqrt(n)) is 5 / 9 / 13.
198 | # The ranges below are deliberately WIDE enough that adjacent settings land
199 | # in different regimes; a finer range would just print duplicate rows.
200 | 
201 | # microcluster.config.MIN_SCOPE_POPULATION -- privacy gate: n >= this.
202 | # Regimes, given scope populations 25 / 75 / 150:
203 | #   20  -> suite (n=25) still allowed        -> finest disclosure = SUITE
204 | #   50  -> suite out, floor (n=75) allowed   -> finest = FLOOR
205 | #   100 -> floor out, building (n=150) only  -> finest = BUILDING
206 | #   200 -> nothing passes the privacy gate   -> nothing is ever disclosed
207 | # 20 is the shipped default.
208 | MIN_SCOPE_POPULATION_SWEEP: tuple[int, ...] = (20, 50, 100, 200)
209 | 
210 | # microcluster.config.STATISTICAL_GATE_FLOOR -- statistical gate:
211 | # qualifying >= max(this, ceil(sqrt(n))). Below ceil(sqrt(25))=5 it does
212 | # nothing to suite-level claims (5 is the shipped default). Each step up
213 | # from there forces disclosure coarser and later: 8 and 12 keep it at
214 | # FLOOR level but demand more evidence first; 16 and 25 push it to
215 | # BUILDING. A suite is fraction-capped at 12 reports, so any floor >= 13
216 | # makes suite-level disclosure impossible outright.
217 | STATISTICAL_GATE_FLOOR_SWEEP: tuple[int, ...] = (5, 8, 12, 16, 25)
218 | 
219 | # microcluster.config.MAX_REPORT_FRACTION -- privacy gate:
220 | # qualifying / n <= this. For a suite (n=25) the allowed count is
221 | # floor(25 * fraction). Only three regimes exist in this scenario:
222 | #   0.08 -> floor(2): even a floor cannot be named -> nothing disclosed
223 | #   0.15 -> floor(3): suite forbidden (needs >= 5)  -> finest = FLOOR
224 | #   0.25 -> floor(6): suite allowed                 -> finest = SUITE
225 | # The shipped default (0.5 -> floor(12)) lands in the SAME regime as 0.25:
226 | # suite report fractions never climb anywhere near it, so tightening from
227 | # 0.5 down to ~0.2 changes nothing. It only starts to bite below that.
228 | MAX_REPORT_FRACTION_SWEEP: tuple[float, ...] = (0.08, 0.15, 0.25)
229 | 
230 | 
231 | # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
232 | # Benchmark (simulation.benchmark): learned detector vs the authored rules
233 | # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
234 | #
235 | # The benchmark builds a labelled dataset from simulated runs -- one row
236 | # per (run, day), features taken from microcluster.detection's three plain
237 | # findings, label taken from ground truth -- trains a small classifier on
238 | # it, and scores that classifier against the hand-authored threshold rules
239 | # in microcluster/config.py + detection.py on HELD-OUT runs. The model
240 | # never replaces the authored rules; it is only ever scored beside them.
241 | #
242 | # Like every other figure in this file these are CHOSEN, not measured.
243 | 
244 | # Fraction of simulation RUNS (never days -- adjacent days from one
245 | # outbreak are correlated and must not straddle the split) held out for
246 | # evaluation.
247 | BENCHMARK_TEST_RUN_FRACTION: float = 0.30
248 | 
249 | # Fixed seed for the train/test run split, so the benchmark is reproducible.
250 | BENCHMARK_SPLIT_SEED: int = 20260906
251 | 
252 | # Max depth of the shallow decision tree (the legible alternative to
253 | # logistic regression). Kept small on purpose: a deep tree just memorises
254 | # this simulator.
255 | BENCHMARK_TREE_MAX_DEPTH: int = 3
256 | 
257 | # Default number of seeds the benchmark simulates for its in-distribution
258 | # dataset.
259 | BENCHMARK_DEFAULT_SEEDS: int = 300
260 | 
261 | # CROSS-REGIME TEST. A model trained on this simulator learns this
262 | # simulator. To see whether it learned anything transferable, it is also
263 | # evaluated on runs generated with a DIFFERENT suite transmission
264 | # probability than it trained on. Training uses the shipped
265 | # SUITE_TRANSMISSION_PROBABILITY; evaluation additionally uses each of
266 | # these (a slower and a faster epidemic).
267 | BENCHMARK_TRAIN_SUITE_TRANSMISSION: float = SUITE_TRANSMISSION_PROBABILITY
268 | BENCHMARK_TEST_SUITE_TRANSMISSIONS: tuple[float, ...] = (0.005, 0.012)
269 | 
270 | 
271 | # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
272 | # Default structure: 1 campus > 1 building > 2 floors > 3 suites > 25 agents
273 | # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
274 | 
275 | # 1 * 2 * 3 * 25 = 150 agents. Declared populations then come out as
276 | # suite n=25, floor n=75, building n=150, campus n=150.
277 | DEFAULT_BUILDINGS: int = 1
278 | DEFAULT_FLOORS_PER_BUILDING: int = 2
279 | DEFAULT_SUITES_PER_FLOOR: int = 3
280 | DEFAULT_AGENTS_PER_SUITE: int = 25
281 | 
282 | # Scope id and display label for the single campus root. Building / floor /
283 | # suite ids are derived from this by the population builder.
284 | CAMPUS_SCOPE_ID: str = "C"
285 | CAMPUS_LABEL: str = "Campus"
286 | 
287 | 
288 | # Which reporting process generates the reports. "clean" is the shipped
289 | # default and the one every committed result was produced with: a fixed
290 | # per-agent probability, independent and memoryless. "realistic" is the
291 | # stress-test alternative in ``simulation.realism`` (heterogeneous baselines,
292 | # correlated reporting, stigma suppression after a group is named). It exists
293 | # to test how much the detector leans on the clean assumption; it is not a
294 | # claim about real people either -- its parameters are chosen, not measured.
295 | REPORTING_MODEL_CLEAN = "clean"
296 | REPORTING_MODEL_REALISTIC = "realistic"
297 | REPORTING_MODELS = (REPORTING_MODEL_CLEAN, REPORTING_MODEL_REALISTIC)
298 | 
299 | 
300 | @dataclass(frozen=True)
301 | class SimulationConfig:
302 |     """Tunable inputs to one simulation run. Defaults come from the module
303 |     constants above; override per run for experiments."""
304 | 
305 |     incubation_days_min: int = INCUBATION_DAYS_MIN
306 |     incubation_days_max: int = INCUBATION_DAYS_MAX
307 |     symptomatic_days_min: int = SYMPTOMATIC_DAYS_MIN
308 |     symptomatic_days_max: int = SYMPTOMATIC_DAYS_MAX
309 |     suite_transmission_probability: float = SUITE_TRANSMISSION_PROBABILITY
310 |     floor_transmission_probability: float = FLOOR_TRANSMISSION_PROBABILITY
311 |     building_transmission_probability: float = BUILDING_TRANSMISSION_PROBABILITY
312 |     reporting_probability_min: float = REPORTING_PROBABILITY_MIN
313 |     reporting_probability_max: float = REPORTING_PROBABILITY_MAX
314 |     outbreak_category: Category = OUTBREAK_CATEGORY
315 |     background_noise_daily_rate: float = BACKGROUND_NOISE_DAILY_RATE
316 |     simulation_start: datetime = SIMULATION_START
317 |     seed_infections: int = DEFAULT_SEED_INFECTIONS
318 |     campus_scope_id: str = CAMPUS_SCOPE_ID
319 |     campus_label: str = CAMPUS_LABEL
320 |     reporting_model: str = REPORTING_MODEL_CLEAN
321 | 
322 |     def __post_init__(self) -> None:
323 |         for lo, hi, name in (
324 |             (self.incubation_days_min, self.incubation_days_max, "incubation_days"),
325 |             (self.symptomatic_days_min, self.symptomatic_days_max, "symptomatic_days"),
326 |             (
327 |                 self.reporting_probability_min,
328 |                 self.reporting_probability_max,
329 |                 "reporting_probability",
330 |             ),
331 |         ):
332 |             if lo > hi:
333 |                 raise ValueError(f"{name}: min {lo!r} exceeds max {hi!r}")
334 |         if self.incubation_days_min < 1 or self.symptomatic_days_min < 1:
335 |             raise ValueError("state durations must be at least 1 day")
336 |         for probability in (
337 |             self.suite_transmission_probability,
338 |             self.floor_transmission_probability,
339 |             self.building_transmission_probability,
340 |             self.reporting_probability_min,
341 |             self.reporting_probability_max,
342 |             self.background_noise_daily_rate,
343 |         ):
344 |             if not 0.0 <= probability <= 1.0:
345 |                 raise ValueError(f"probability {probability!r} must be in [0, 1]")
346 |         if not isinstance(self.outbreak_category, Category):
347 |             raise ValueError(f"outbreak_category must be a Category, got {self.outbreak_category!r}")
348 |         if self.seed_infections < 0:
349 |             raise ValueError("seed_infections must be >= 0")
350 |         if self.reporting_model not in REPORTING_MODELS:
351 |             raise ValueError(
352 |                 f"reporting_model must be one of {REPORTING_MODELS}, got {self.reporting_model!r}"
353 |             )
354 | 
355 | 
356 | DEFAULT_SIMULATION_CONFIG = SimulationConfig()

simulation/contact.py

  1 | """Contact model: tiered transmission on a single day.
  2 | 
  3 | Contact is not suite-only. An infectious agent can transmit to a
  4 | susceptible agent that shares its SUITE, its FLOOR (different suite, same
  5 | floor), or its BUILDING (different floor, same building) -- at three
  6 | decreasing per-contact probabilities. Which scope an agent's suite sits
  7 | under is read from the ``ScopeRegistry`` (see :func:`suite_ancestor_ids`),
  8 | never hard-coded from id strings.
  9 | """
 10 | 
 11 | from __future__ import annotations
 12 | 
 13 | import random
 14 | from collections import Counter
 15 | 
 16 | from microcluster.models import ScopeLevel, ScopeRegistry
 17 | 
 18 | from .agent import Agent
 19 | from .config import SimulationConfig
 20 | from .infection import INFECTIOUS_STATES, InfectionState
 21 | 
 22 | 
 23 | def suite_ancestor_ids(registry: ScopeRegistry, suite_id: str) -> tuple[str, str]:
 24 |     """Return ``(floor_id, building_id)`` for ``suite_id``, read from the
 25 |     registry's own hierarchy rather than assumed from id strings.
 26 | 
 27 |     If the registry has no FLOOR scope above this suite, ``floor_id``
 28 |     falls back to ``suite_id`` itself (so the floor tier contributes
 29 |     nothing, rather than guessing a structure that is not there). If it
 30 |     also has no BUILDING scope, ``building_id`` falls back to whatever
 31 |     ``floor_id`` resolved to, for the same reason.
 32 |     """
 33 |     by_level = {scope.level: scope.id for scope in registry.chain_to_root(suite_id)}
 34 |     floor_id = by_level.get(ScopeLevel.FLOOR, suite_id)
 35 |     building_id = by_level.get(ScopeLevel.BUILDING, floor_id)
 36 |     return floor_id, building_id
 37 | 
 38 | 
 39 | def new_infections(
 40 |     agents_by_suite: dict[str, list[Agent]],
 41 |     floor_of_suite: dict[str, str],
 42 |     building_of_suite: dict[str, str],
 43 |     rng: random.Random,
 44 |     config: SimulationConfig,
 45 | ) -> tuple[list[Agent], list[Agent]]:
 46 |     """Given every agent grouped by suite and their CURRENT states, return
 47 |     the susceptible agents that become infected today, across all three
 48 |     contact tiers, as ``(within_suite, cross_suite)``.
 49 | 
 50 |     For a susceptible agent in a given suite, every infectious agent is one
 51 |     of three kinds of contact: a suite-mate, a floor-mate in a different
 52 |     suite, or a building-mate on a different floor. The agent escapes
 53 |     infection only by escaping every contact at every tier, so with
 54 |     per-contact probabilities ``p_suite``, ``p_floor``, ``p_building`` and
 55 |     contact counts ``k_suite``, ``k_floor``, ``k_building``:
 56 | 
 57 |         P(infected today) = 1 - (1-p_suite)**k_suite
 58 |                                * (1-p_floor)**k_floor
 59 |                                * (1-p_building)**k_building
 60 | 
 61 |     All susceptible agents within the same suite face the same probability
 62 |     (the three counts are suite/floor/building aggregates, not
 63 |     per-individual), so it is computed once per suite.
 64 | 
 65 |     A newly infected agent is reported as ``cross_suite`` if their OWN
 66 |     suite had zero infectious members (``k_suite == 0``) -- the infection
 67 |     can then only have come from a floor- or building-tier contact, never
 68 |     a suite-mate. Otherwise it is reported as ``within_suite``: its suite
 69 |     did have an infectious member, even though a floor/building contact
 70 |     may also have contributed to the same compounded probability (the
 71 |     tiers are not disentangled at the level of one infection event).
 72 |     """
 73 |     infectious_by_suite: dict[str, int] = {
 74 |         suite_id: sum(1 for a in agents if a.state in INFECTIOUS_STATES)
 75 |         for suite_id, agents in agents_by_suite.items()
 76 |     }
 77 | 
 78 |     infectious_by_floor: Counter[str] = Counter()
 79 |     infectious_by_building: Counter[str] = Counter()
 80 |     for suite_id, count in infectious_by_suite.items():
 81 |         if count:
 82 |             infectious_by_floor[floor_of_suite[suite_id]] += count
 83 |             infectious_by_building[building_of_suite[suite_id]] += count
 84 | 
 85 |     p_suite = config.suite_transmission_probability
 86 |     p_floor = config.floor_transmission_probability
 87 |     p_building = config.building_transmission_probability
 88 | 
 89 |     within_suite: list[Agent] = []
 90 |     cross_suite: list[Agent] = []
 91 |     for suite_id, agents in agents_by_suite.items():
 92 |         floor_id = floor_of_suite[suite_id]
 93 |         building_id = building_of_suite[suite_id]
 94 | 
 95 |         k_suite = infectious_by_suite[suite_id]
 96 |         k_floor = infectious_by_floor[floor_id] - k_suite
 97 |         k_building = infectious_by_building[building_id] - infectious_by_floor[floor_id]
 98 | 
 99 |         if k_suite == 0 and k_floor == 0 and k_building == 0:
100 |             continue
101 | 
102 |         escape = (
103 |             (1.0 - p_suite) ** k_suite
104 |             * (1.0 - p_floor) ** k_floor
105 |             * (1.0 - p_building) ** k_building
106 |         )
107 |         prob_infected = 1.0 - escape
108 |         bucket = within_suite if k_suite > 0 else cross_suite
109 | 
110 |         bucket.extend(
111 |             agent
112 |             for agent in agents
113 |             if agent.state is InfectionState.SUSCEPTIBLE
114 |             and rng.random() < prob_infected
115 |         )
116 |     return within_suite, cross_suite

simulation/detection_demo.py

  1 | """End-to-end demo: simulate an outbreak, generate anonymous reports from
  2 | it, and feed them to the UNCHANGED detection engine, once per simulated
  3 | day.
  4 | 
  5 |     python -m simulation.detection_demo
  6 | 
  7 | Prints, per seed and per day: the true infection count (ground truth --
  8 | never seen by the detector), how many reports were submitted that day,
  9 | whether detection fired, which detector, and the disclosed scope if any.
 10 | Then a summary of TWO delays -- time-to-first-FIRE and
 11 | time-to-first-DISCLOSURE -- and the full list of distinct scopes disclosed
 12 | over the run (scope stability, config.SIBLING_SWITCH_MARGIN, keeps this
 13 | list short).
 14 | """
 15 | 
 16 | from __future__ import annotations
 17 | 
 18 | import sys
 19 | from pathlib import Path
 20 | 
 21 | sys.path.insert(0, str(Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent))
 22 | 
 23 | from simulation.config import DEFAULT_RUN_DAYS, DEFAULT_SMOKE_SEEDS  # noqa: E402
 24 | from simulation.pipeline import DailyRecord, run_with_detection  # noqa: E402
 25 | 
 26 | 
 27 | def _detector_label(record: DailyRecord) -> str:
 28 |     if record.relative_fired and record.absolute_fired:
 29 |         return "both"
 30 |     if record.relative_fired:
 31 |         return "relative"
 32 |     if record.absolute_fired:
 33 |         return "absolute"
 34 |     return "-"
 35 | 
 36 | 
 37 | def _print_run(seed: int, days: int) -> None:
 38 |     result = run_with_detection(seed=seed, days=days)
 39 | 
 40 |     header = (
 41 |         f"{'day':>3}  {'true_infected':>13}  {'reports_today':>13}  "
 42 |         f"{'fired':>5}  {'detector':>8}  disclosed_scope"
 43 |     )
 44 |     print(f"SEED {seed}")
 45 |     print(header)
 46 |     print("-" * len(header))
 47 | 
 48 |     outbreak_started_day: int | None = None
 49 | 
 50 |     for record in result.daily_records:
 51 |         if outbreak_started_day is None and record.true_infected_count > 0:
 52 |             outbreak_started_day = record.day
 53 | 
 54 |         print(
 55 |             f"{record.day:>3}  {record.true_infected_count:>13}  "
 56 |             f"{record.reports_submitted_today:>13}  "
 57 |             f"{str(record.detection_fired):>5}  {_detector_label(record):>8}  "
 58 |             f"{record.disclosed_scope_id or '-'}"
 59 |         )
 60 | 
 61 |     last = result.daily_records[-1]
 62 |     fired_day = last.first_fired_day
 63 |     disclosed_day = last.first_disclosed_day
 64 |     distinct_scopes = result.distinct_disclosed_scopes()
 65 | 
 66 |     print()
 67 |     if outbreak_started_day is None:
 68 |         print("  outbreak never took hold: nobody was ever infected")
 69 |     else:
 70 |         print(f"  outbreak began (ground truth)   : day {outbreak_started_day}")
 71 | 
 72 |     if fired_day is None:
 73 |         print("  detector never fired in the run")
 74 |     else:
 75 |         print(f"  time-to-first-FIRE              : day {fired_day}"
 76 |               + (f"  (delay {fired_day - outbreak_started_day} day(s) from outbreak start)"
 77 |                  if outbreak_started_day is not None else ""))
 78 | 
 79 |     if disclosed_day is None:
 80 |         print("  detector never disclosed a scope in the run")
 81 |     else:
 82 |         print(f"  time-to-first-DISCLOSURE        : day {disclosed_day}"
 83 |               + (f"  (delay {disclosed_day - outbreak_started_day} day(s) from outbreak start)"
 84 |                  if outbreak_started_day is not None else ""))
 85 | 
 86 |     if fired_day is not None and disclosed_day is not None:
 87 |         print(f"  cost of the privacy policy      : {disclosed_day - fired_day} day(s) "
 88 |               f"(fired but could not yet be described)")
 89 | 
 90 |     print(f"  distinct scopes ever disclosed  : {len(distinct_scopes)} {distinct_scopes}")
 91 |     print()
 92 | 
 93 | 
 94 | def main() -> None:
 95 |     for seed in DEFAULT_SMOKE_SEEDS:
 96 |         _print_run(seed, DEFAULT_RUN_DAYS)
 97 | 
 98 | 
 99 | if __name__ == "__main__":
100 |     main()

simulation/experiments.py

  1 | """Experiment runner: statistics over many simulated outbreaks.
  2 | 
  3 | Still headless -- no plotting, no API, no frontend. Runs the pipeline
  4 | across many random seeds, sweeping the DISCLOSURE gates one at a time,
  5 | and prints the delay-versus-infections relationship as a plain data
  6 | table.
  7 | 
  8 |     python -m simulation.experiments
  9 | 
 10 | ---------------------------------------------------------------------
 11 | WHY THE DISCLOSURE GATES, NOT THE DETECTION THRESHOLD
 12 | ---------------------------------------------------------------------
 13 | 
 14 | An earlier version of this runner swept ``ABSOLUTE_EXCESS_RATIO`` (the
 15 | detection threshold) and found time-to-disclosure completely invariant to
 16 | it -- 14.1 days across every setting. The detector fires well before the
 17 | disclosure engine will name anything anyway, so it is the DISCLOSURE
 18 | gates, not detection, that set the timing of the first public statement.
 19 | So this runner sweeps those, holding detection config at its defaults.
 20 | 
 21 | Each disclosure gate ultimately compares an INTEGER qualifying-report
 22 | count (or a declared population) against a threshold, so a parameter only
 23 | changes behaviour when it crosses a discrete boundary. In the default
 24 | structure the scope populations are 25 / 75 / 150 (suite / floor /
 25 | building = campus, one building) and ``ceil(sqrt(n))`` is 5 / 9 / 13. The
 26 | swept ranges (``config.*_SWEEP``) are deliberately wide enough that
 27 | adjacent settings land in different regimes; ``adjacent_duplicate_rows``
 28 | flags any that still collapse to an identical row.
 29 | 
 30 | ---------------------------------------------------------------------
 31 | METHODOLOGICAL CHOICE: establishment conditioning
 32 | ---------------------------------------------------------------------
 33 | 
 34 | A single seeded index case is a stochastic branching process (see
 35 | ``simulation/config.py`` next to ``SUITE_TRANSMISSION_PROBABILITY``): a
 36 | real fraction of runs fail to take off at all, purely by chance, before
 37 | the detector or the disclosure policy ever get a say. An outbreak counts
 38 | as ESTABLISHED once its cumulative (ever-infected) case count reaches
 39 | ``config.ESTABLISHED_MIN_INFECTIONS``.
 40 | 
 41 | Mixing fizzled runs in with established ones would swamp every other
 42 | statistic with take-off variance. Standard practice in stochastic
 43 | epidemic simulation: report the fade-out (fizzle) rate on its own, then
 44 | condition every other statistic on establishment.
 45 | 
 46 | The one number NOT conditioned on establishment is the FALSE-ALARM RATE:
 47 | the fraction of NON-established runs where the detector fired anyway. It
 48 | is the cost side of a loose detector and belongs in the headline results,
 49 | not a footnote. Detection is fixed here, so it is a single number.
 50 | 
 51 | ---------------------------------------------------------------------
 52 | READING ``gap`` (policy cost)
 53 | ---------------------------------------------------------------------
 54 | 
 55 | ``gap = disclosure_delay - fire_delay``. On its own it is ambiguous: it
 56 | shrinks when disclosure gets FASTER (good) or when detection gets SLOWER
 57 | (bad). Both delays are printed side by side, over the same subset of runs,
 58 | so the two cases cannot be confused -- and because detection config is
 59 | held fixed across every sweep, ``fire_delay`` is the control: any movement
 60 | in ``gap`` between rows is disclosure timing, not detection.
 61 | """
 62 | 
 63 | from __future__ import annotations
 64 | 
 65 | import math
 66 | import sys
 67 | from dataclasses import dataclass
 68 | from pathlib import Path
 69 | from statistics import mean
 70 | from typing import Callable, Iterable, Sequence
 71 | 
 72 | sys.path.insert(0, str(Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent))
 73 | 
 74 | from microcluster.config import (  # noqa: E402
 75 |     DEFAULT_DETECTION_CONFIG,
 76 |     DEFAULT_DISCLOSURE_CONFIG,
 77 |     DisclosureConfig,
 78 | )
 79 | from microcluster.disclosure import statistical_threshold  # noqa: E402
 80 | from microcluster.models import ScopeLevel  # noqa: E402
 81 | 
 82 | from simulation.config import (  # noqa: E402
 83 |     DEFAULT_EXPERIMENT_SEEDS,
 84 |     DEFAULT_RUN_DAYS,
 85 |     ESTABLISHED_MIN_INFECTIONS,
 86 |     MAX_REPORT_FRACTION_SWEEP,
 87 |     MIN_SCOPE_POPULATION_SWEEP,
 88 |     STATISTICAL_GATE_FLOOR_SWEEP,
 89 | )
 90 | from simulation.infection import InfectionState  # noqa: E402
 91 | from simulation.pipeline import (  # noqa: E402
 92 |     DailyRecord,
 93 |     SimulatedReports,
 94 |     analyze_report_stream,
 95 |     run_with_detection,
 96 |     simulate_and_report,
 97 | )
 98 | from simulation.simulator import Simulation  # noqa: E402
 99 | 
100 | __all__ = [
101 |     "RunOutcome",
102 |     "SettingSummary",
103 |     "DisclosureSweep",
104 |     "SweepResults",
105 |     "run_once",
106 |     "reduce_run",
107 |     "summarize_setting",
108 |     "fizzle_rate",
109 |     "false_alarm_rate_at_defaults",
110 |     "overall_mean_fire_delay",

112 |     "degenerate_reason_for",
113 |     "run_disclosure_sweeps",
114 |     "DISCLOSURE_SWEEPS",

117 | 
118 | def degenerate_reason_for(
119 |     config: DisclosureConfig, scope_populations: Iterable[int]
120 | ) -> str | None:
121 |     """If NO scope in ``scope_populations`` can pass both gates at ANY
122 |     achievable qualifying count under ``config``, return a one-line reason
123 |     the configuration is structurally impossible; otherwise ``None``.
124 | 
125 |     This is not a data point -- it is a setting under which the disclosure
126 |     engine can never say anything regardless of the outbreak. A scope of
127 |     declared population ``n`` is disclosable for some count iff::
128 | 
129 |         n >= min_scope_population
130 |         and  max(statistical_gate_floor, ceil(sqrt(n)))  <=  floor(n * max_report_fraction)
131 | 
132 |     (the statistical gate's requirement must fit under the privacy gate's
133 |     fraction cap). MIN_SCOPE_POPULATION greater than the whole campus is
134 |     the obvious case; a very low MAX_REPORT_FRACTION is another.
135 |     """
136 |     pops = sorted(set(scope_populations))
137 |     for n in pops:
138 |         if n < config.min_scope_population:
139 |             continue
140 |         need = statistical_threshold(n, config)
141 |         allowed = math.floor(n * config.max_report_fraction)
142 |         if need <= allowed:
143 |             return None
144 |     biggest = pops[-1] if pops else 0
145 |     if all(n < config.min_scope_population for n in pops):
146 |         return (
147 |             f"min_scope_population={config.min_scope_population} exceeds every "
148 |             f"declared scope population (largest is {biggest}); the privacy "
149 |             f"gate can never pass"
150 |         )
151 |     return (
152 |         f"no scope population in {pops} admits a qualifying count that clears "
153 |         f"the statistical gate while staying under max_report_fraction="
154 |         f"{config.max_report_fraction:g}"
155 |     )
156 | 
157 | 
158 | # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
159 | # Per-run outcome
160 | # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
161 | 
162 | 
163 | @dataclass(frozen=True)
164 | class RunOutcome:
165 |     """Ground truth and detection/disclosure outcome for one simulated seed."""
166 | 
167 |     seed: int
168 |     established: bool
169 |     total_infections: int  # cumulative ever-infected at the end of the run (day 30)
170 |     peak_infected: int  # largest simultaneous (INCUBATING + SYMPTOMATIC) count
171 |     first_fired_day: int | None
172 |     first_disclosed_day: int | None
173 |     infections_at_first_disclosure: int | None  # cumulative ever-infected on that day
174 |     finest_disclosed_level: ScopeLevel | None  # deepest level named anywhere in the run
175 | 
176 |     @property
177 |     def policy_cost(self) -> int | None:
178 |         if self.first_fired_day is None or self.first_disclosed_day is None:
179 |             return None
180 |         return self.first_disclosed_day - self.first_fired_day
181 | 
182 | 
183 | def _cumulative_infected(simulation: Simulation, day_index: int) -> int:
184 |     snapshot = simulation.history[day_index]
185 |     return sum(1 for a in snapshot.agents if a.state is not InfectionState.SUSCEPTIBLE)
186 | 
187 | 
188 | def reduce_run(
189 |     seed: int,
190 |     simulation: Simulation,
191 |     daily_records: list[DailyRecord],
192 |     *,
193 |     established_min_infections: int = ESTABLISHED_MIN_INFECTIONS,
194 | ) -> RunOutcome:
195 |     """Collapse one simulated + analysed run into a :class:`RunOutcome`."""
196 |     total_infections = _cumulative_infected(simulation, -1)
197 |     peak_infected = max(
198 |         s.count(InfectionState.INCUBATING) + s.count(InfectionState.SYMPTOMATIC)
199 |         for s in simulation.history
200 |     )
201 | 
202 |     last = daily_records[-1]
203 |     fire_day = last.first_fired_day
204 |     disclose_day = last.first_disclosed_day
205 | 
206 |     infections_at_disclosure = (
207 |         _cumulative_infected(simulation, disclose_day) if disclose_day is not None else None
208 |     )
209 | 
210 |     finest_level: ScopeLevel | None = None
211 |     for record in daily_records:
212 |         if record.disclosed_scope_id is None:
213 |             continue
214 |         level = simulation.registry.get(record.disclosed_scope_id).level
215 |         finest_level = level if finest_level is None else max(finest_level, level)
216 | 
217 |     return RunOutcome(
218 |         seed=seed,
219 |         established=total_infections >= established_min_infections,
220 |         total_infections=total_infections,
221 |         peak_infected=peak_infected,
222 |         first_fired_day=fire_day,
223 |         first_disclosed_day=disclose_day,
224 |         infections_at_first_disclosure=infections_at_disclosure,
225 |         finest_disclosed_level=finest_level,
226 |     )
227 | 
228 | 
229 | def run_once(
230 |     seed: int,
231 |     *,
232 |     days: int = DEFAULT_RUN_DAYS,
233 |     detection_config=DEFAULT_DETECTION_CONFIG,
234 |     disclosure_config: DisclosureConfig = DEFAULT_DISCLOSURE_CONFIG,
235 |     established_min_infections: int = ESTABLISHED_MIN_INFECTIONS,
236 | ) -> RunOutcome:
237 |     """Run one seed end to end (convenience wrapper over the pipeline)."""
238 |     run = run_with_detection(
239 |         seed=seed,
240 |         days=days,
241 |         detection_config=detection_config,
242 |         disclosure_config=disclosure_config,
243 |     )
244 |     return reduce_run(
245 |         seed,
246 |         run.simulation,
247 |         run.daily_records,
248 |         established_min_infections=established_min_infections,
249 |     )
250 | 
251 | 
252 | # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
253 | # Aggregation for one sweep setting
254 | # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
255 | 
256 | 
257 | def _mean(values: list[float]) -> float | None:
258 |     return mean(values) if values else None
259 | 
260 | 
261 | @dataclass(frozen=True)
262 | class SettingSummary:
263 |     """Aggregate statistics for one value of one swept disclosure parameter.
264 | 
265 |     ``mean_*`` fields are over ESTABLISHED runs only (the disclosure-timing
266 |     ones additionally only over established runs that actually disclosed).
267 |     ``false_alarm_rate`` is over NON-established runs only.
268 |     """
269 | 
270 |     param_name: str
271 |     value: float
272 | 
273 |     n_runs: int
274 |     n_established: int
275 |     n_non_established: int
276 |     n_fired: int  # established runs that ever fired  (detection-only; constant per sweep)
277 |     n_disclosed: int  # established runs that ever disclosed  (varies with the parameter)
278 | 
279 |     mean_total_infections: float | None  # day-30 cumulative; detection/disclosure-independent
280 | 
281 |     # All four over the SAME subset: established runs that disclosed.
282 |     mean_fire_delay: float | None
283 |     mean_disclosure_delay: float | None
284 |     mean_gap: float | None  # disclosure_delay - fire_delay  == mean policy cost
285 |     mean_infections_at_disclosure: float | None  # CONDITIONAL on disclosure (survivorship)
286 | 
287 |     # Over ALL established runs, disclosed or not: infections that were
288 |     # never disclosed before the run ended. A run that disclosed
289 |     # contributes its infection count at first disclosure; a run that never
290 |     # disclosed contributes its full day-30 total (none of its infections
291 |     # were ever disclosed). This is the unbiased companion to
292 |     # ``mean_infections_at_disclosure``.
293 |     mean_infections_before_disclosure_unbiased: float | None
294 |     disclosed_fraction: float | None  # n_disclosed / n_established -- the selection made visible
295 | 
296 |     finest_disclosed_level: ScopeLevel | None
297 | 
298 |     n_false_alarms: int
299 |     false_alarm_rate: float | None
300 | 
301 |     # A structurally impossible configuration (see degenerate_reason_for):
302 |     # no scope can pass both gates at any count. Not a data point.
303 |     degenerate: bool = False
304 |     degenerate_reason: str = ""
305 | 
306 |     def regime_key(self) -> tuple:
307 |         """The disclosure-dependent outcome, for spotting duplicate regimes
308 |         (adjacent settings the integer arithmetic did not separate)."""
309 |         return (
310 |             self.n_disclosed,
311 |             self.mean_disclosure_delay,
312 |             self.mean_gap,
313 |             self.mean_infections_at_disclosure,
314 |             self.finest_disclosed_level,
315 |         )
316 | 
317 | 
318 | def summarize_setting(
319 |     outcomes: list[RunOutcome],
320 |     *,
321 |     param_name: str,
322 |     value: float,
323 |     degenerate: bool = False,
324 |     degenerate_reason: str = "",
325 | ) -> SettingSummary:
326 |     established = [o for o in outcomes if o.established]
327 |     non_established = [o for o in outcomes if not o.established]
328 | 
329 |     fired = [o for o in established if o.first_fired_day is not None]
330 |     disclosed = [o for o in established if o.first_disclosed_day is not None]
331 |     false_alarms = [o for o in non_established if o.first_fired_day is not None]
332 | 
333 |     # Unbiased: every established run contributes, a non-disclosing one at
334 |     # its full day-30 total (none of its infections were ever disclosed).
335 |     infections_before_disclosure = [
336 |         o.infections_at_first_disclosure
337 |         if o.first_disclosed_day is not None
338 |         else o.total_infections
339 |         for o in established
340 |     ]
341 | 
342 |     finest: ScopeLevel | None = None
343 |     for o in disclosed:
344 |         if o.finest_disclosed_level is not None:
345 |             finest = (
346 |                 o.finest_disclosed_level
347 |                 if finest is None
348 |                 else max(finest, o.finest_disclosed_level)
349 |             )
350 | 
351 |     return SettingSummary(
352 |         param_name=param_name,
353 |         value=value,
354 |         n_runs=len(outcomes),
355 |         n_established=len(established),
356 |         n_non_established=len(non_established),
357 |         n_fired=len(fired),
358 |         n_disclosed=len(disclosed),
359 |         mean_total_infections=_mean([o.total_infections for o in established]),
360 |         mean_fire_delay=_mean([o.first_fired_day for o in disclosed]),
361 |         mean_disclosure_delay=_mean([o.first_disclosed_day for o in disclosed]),
362 |         mean_gap=_mean([o.policy_cost for o in disclosed]),
363 |         mean_infections_at_disclosure=_mean(
364 |             [o.infections_at_first_disclosure for o in disclosed]
365 |         ),
366 |         mean_infections_before_disclosure_unbiased=_mean(infections_before_disclosure),
367 |         disclosed_fraction=(len(disclosed) / len(established)) if established else None,
368 |         finest_disclosed_level=finest,
369 |         n_false_alarms=len(false_alarms),
370 |         false_alarm_rate=(len(false_alarms) / len(non_established)) if non_established else None,
371 |         degenerate=degenerate,
372 |         degenerate_reason=degenerate_reason,
373 |     )
374 | 
375 | 
376 | def fizzle_rate(outcomes: list[RunOutcome]) -> float:
377 |     """Fraction of runs that never established. Independent of both engine
378 |     configs -- reported once, not per row."""
379 |     if not outcomes:
380 |         return 0.0
381 |     return 1 - sum(o.established for o in outcomes) / len(outcomes)
382 | 
383 | 
384 | def false_alarm_rate_at_defaults(outcomes: list[RunOutcome]) -> tuple[int, int, float | None]:
385 |     """(n_false_alarms, n_non_established, rate) over a baseline (default
386 |     config) outcome set. Detection is fixed for every sweep, so this
387 |     single number is the false-alarm rate everywhere below."""
388 |     non_established = [o for o in outcomes if not o.established]
389 |     alarms = [o for o in non_established if o.first_fired_day is not None]
390 |     rate = (len(alarms) / len(non_established)) if non_established else None
391 |     return len(alarms), len(non_established), rate
392 | 
393 | 
394 | def overall_mean_fire_delay(outcomes: list[RunOutcome]) -> float | None:
395 |     """Mean fire delay over ALL established runs that fired (not just the
396 |     ones that went on to disclose). The detection-side control value."""
397 |     return _mean(
398 |         [o.first_fired_day for o in outcomes if o.established and o.first_fired_day is not None]
399 |     )
400 | 
401 | 
402 | def adjacent_duplicate_rows(summaries: list[SettingSummary]) -> list[tuple[float, float]]:
403 |     """Pairs of adjacent setting values whose disclosure-dependent outcome
404 |     is identical -- the range is too fine for the integer arithmetic
405 |     underneath at that point."""
406 |     dupes: list[tuple[float, float]] = []
407 |     for earlier, later in zip(summaries, summaries[1:]):
408 |         # Degenerate rows are labelled IMPOSSIBLE in the output, not
409 |         # compared as data -- two of them side by side is not a
410 |         # duplicate-regime problem.
411 |         if earlier.degenerate or later.degenerate:
412 |             continue
413 |         if earlier.regime_key() == later.regime_key():
414 |             dupes.append((earlier.value, later.value))
415 |     return dupes
416 | 
417 | 
418 | # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
419 | # The three sweeps
420 | # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
421 | 
422 | 
423 | @dataclass(frozen=True)
424 | class DisclosureSweep:
425 |     param_name: str
426 |     default_value: float  # the shipped default, for context in the output
427 |     values: tuple
428 |     config_for: Callable[[float], DisclosureConfig]
429 |     note: str = ""
430 | 
431 | 
432 | DISCLOSURE_SWEEPS: tuple[DisclosureSweep, ...] = (
433 |     DisclosureSweep(
434 |         "MIN_SCOPE_POPULATION",
435 |         20,
436 |         MIN_SCOPE_POPULATION_SWEEP,
437 |         lambda v: DisclosureConfig(min_scope_population=int(v)),
438 |         note="privacy gate n >= this; scope populations are 25 / 75 / 150.",
439 |     ),
440 |     DisclosureSweep(
441 |         "STATISTICAL_GATE_FLOOR",
442 |         5,
443 |         STATISTICAL_GATE_FLOOR_SWEEP,
444 |         lambda v: DisclosureConfig(statistical_gate_floor=int(v)),
445 |         note="statistical gate qualifying >= max(this, ceil(sqrt(n))); ceil(sqrt) is 5 / 9 / 13.",
446 |     ),
447 |     DisclosureSweep(
448 |         "MAX_REPORT_FRACTION",
449 |         0.5,
450 |         MAX_REPORT_FRACTION_SWEEP,
451 |         lambda v: DisclosureConfig(max_report_fraction=float(v)),
452 |         note=(
453 |             "privacy gate qualifying/n <= this. The shipped default (0.5) lands in "
454 |             "the SAME regime as 0.25 and is not shown: suite report fractions never "
455 |             "approach it, so it never binds. It only bites below ~0.2."
456 |         ),
457 |     ),
458 | )
459 | 
460 | 
461 | @dataclass
462 | class SweepResults:
463 |     fizzle_rate: float
464 |     false_alarms: int
465 |     non_established: int
466 |     false_alarm_rate: float | None
467 |     control_fire_delay: float | None  # mean fire delay, all established fired runs
468 |     n_established: int
469 |     n_fired: int
470 |     mean_total_infections: float | None
471 |     sweeps: dict[str, list[SettingSummary]]
472 | 
473 | 
474 | def run_disclosure_sweeps(
475 |     seeds: Sequence[int] | None = None,
476 |     *,
477 |     n_seeds: int = DEFAULT_EXPERIMENT_SEEDS,
478 |     days: int = DEFAULT_RUN_DAYS,
479 |     sweeps: Sequence[DisclosureSweep] = DISCLOSURE_SWEEPS,
480 | ) -> SweepResults:
481 |     """Simulate every seed ONCE, then replay the analysis for every value
482 |     of every disclosure sweep on that shared report stream."""
483 |     seed_list = list(seeds) if seeds is not None else list(range(1, n_seeds + 1))
484 |     simulated: dict[int, SimulatedReports] = {
485 |         seed: simulate_and_report(seed=seed, days=days) for seed in seed_list
486 |     }
487 | 
488 |     sample_registry = next(iter(simulated.values())).simulation.registry
489 |     scope_populations = tuple(
490 |         sorted({s.population for s in sample_registry.scopes.values()})
491 |     )
492 | 
493 |     def outcomes_for(disclosure_config: DisclosureConfig) -> list[RunOutcome]:
494 |         return [
495 |             reduce_run(
496 |                 seed,
497 |                 sr.simulation,
498 |                 analyze_report_stream(sr, disclosure_config=disclosure_config),
499 |             )
500 |             for seed, sr in simulated.items()
501 |         ]
502 | 
503 |     baseline = outcomes_for(DEFAULT_DISCLOSURE_CONFIG)
504 |     alarms, non_est, alarm_rate = false_alarm_rate_at_defaults(baseline)
505 | 
506 |     sweep_summaries: dict[str, list[SettingSummary]] = {}
507 |     for sweep in sweeps:
508 |         rows: list[SettingSummary] = []
509 |         for value in sweep.values:
510 |             disclosure_config = sweep.config_for(value)
511 |             reason = degenerate_reason_for(disclosure_config, scope_populations)
512 |             rows.append(
513 |                 summarize_setting(
514 |                     outcomes_for(disclosure_config),
515 |                     param_name=sweep.param_name,
516 |                     value=value,
517 |                     degenerate=reason is not None,
518 |                     degenerate_reason=reason or "",
519 |                 )
520 |             )
521 |         sweep_summaries[sweep.param_name] = rows
522 | 
523 |     return SweepResults(
524 |         fizzle_rate=fizzle_rate(baseline),
525 |         false_alarms=alarms,
526 |         non_established=non_est,
527 |         false_alarm_rate=alarm_rate,
528 |         control_fire_delay=overall_mean_fire_delay(baseline),
529 |         n_established=sum(o.established for o in baseline),
530 |         n_fired=sum(
531 |             1 for o in baseline if o.established and o.first_fired_day is not None
532 |         ),
533 |         mean_total_infections=_mean(
534 |             [o.total_infections for o in baseline if o.established]
535 |         ),
536 |         sweeps=sweep_summaries,
537 |     )
538 | 
539 | 
540 | # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
541 | # Reporting
542 | # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
543 | 
544 | 
545 | def _fmt(value: float | None, decimals: int = 1) -> str:
546 |     return "n/a" if value is None else f"{value:.{decimals}f}"
547 | 
548 | 
549 | def _level_name(level: ScopeLevel | None) -> str:
550 |     return "none" if level is None else level.name
551 | 
552 | 
553 | def _print_sweep(sweep: DisclosureSweep, summaries: list[SettingSummary]) -> None:
554 |     print(f"--- Sweep: {sweep.param_name} (default {sweep.default_value:g}; detection fixed) ---")
555 |     if sweep.note:
556 |         print(f"    {sweep.note}")
557 | 
558 |     columns = [
559 |         ("value", 7),
560 |         ("disc_frac", 9),
561 |         ("inf<disc", 9),
562 |         ("inf@disc", 9),
563 |         ("disc_delay", 10),
564 |         ("fire_delay", 10),
565 |         ("gap", 6),
566 |         ("finest_lvl", 10),
567 |     ]
568 |     header = "  ".join(f"{name:>{width}}" for name, width in columns)
569 |     print(header)
570 |     print("-" * len(header))
571 |     for s in summaries:
572 |         if s.degenerate:
573 |             print(f"{s.value:>7g}  IMPOSSIBLE -- {s.degenerate_reason}")
574 |             continue
575 |         frac = (
576 |             "n/a"
577 |             if s.disclosed_fraction is None
578 |             else f"{s.n_disclosed}/{s.n_established}"
579 |         )
580 |         row = [
581 |             f"{s.value:g}",
582 |             frac,
583 |             _fmt(s.mean_infections_before_disclosure_unbiased),
584 |             _fmt(s.mean_infections_at_disclosure),
