tests/test_reporting.py

  1 | """Report generation: the one place simulated agents turn into anonymous
  2 | ``microcluster.models.Report`` objects."""
  3 | 
  4 | from __future__ import annotations
  5 | 
  6 | import random
  7 | import unittest
  8 | 
  9 | from microcluster.models import CHECKLIST, Category
 10 | 
 11 | from simulation.agent import Agent
 12 | from simulation.config import SimulationConfig
 13 | from simulation.infection import InfectionState
 14 | from simulation.reporting import generate_daily_reports
 15 | 
 16 | 
 17 | def _symptomatic_agent(**overrides) -> Agent:
 18 |     kwargs = dict(
 19 |         id="A1",
 20 |         suite_id="S1",
 21 |         reporting_probability=1.0,
 22 |         state=InfectionState.SYMPTOMATIC,
 23 |         state_changed_day=0,
 24 |         incubation_days=2,
 25 |         symptomatic_days=5,
 26 |     )
 27 |     kwargs.update(overrides)
 28 |     return Agent(**kwargs)
 29 |

tests/test_scale_and_realism.py

  1 | """Scale validation and reporting-realism tests.
  2 | 
  3 | Scale: the largest structure that works (2,000 agents, four distinct scope
  4 | populations) must run end to end, keep the evidence gate equal to its
  5 | formula on every evaluation, and still trigger the privacy gate.
  6 | 
  7 | Realism: the alternative reporting model must be strictly opt-in (the
  8 | default config is unchanged and every committed result stays valid), must
  9 | reduce to the clean model when all three effects are off, and each effect
 10 | must push the reporting process in the direction it claims to.
 11 | """
 12 | 
 13 | from __future__ import annotations
 14 | 
 15 | import math
 16 | import unittest
 17 | from dataclasses import replace
 18 | 
 19 | from microcluster.models import ScopeLevel
 20 | from simulation.config import (
 21 |     DEFAULT_SIMULATION_CONFIG,
 22 |     REPORTING_MODEL_CLEAN,
 23 |     REPORTING_MODEL_REALISTIC,
 24 |     SimulationConfig,
 25 | )
 26 | from simulation.pipeline import run_with_detection, simulate_and_report
 27 | from simulation.realism import RealismConfig, run_realistic
 28 | from simulation.scale import SCALE_SPECS, measure_size
 29 | 
 30 | 
 31 | def _sig(run):
 32 |     return [
 33 |         (r.disclosed_scope_id, r.reports_submitted_today, r.detection_fired)
 34 |         for r in run.daily_records
 35 |     ]
 36 | 
 37 | 
 38 | class ScaleTest(unittest.TestCase):
 39 |     def test_2000_agents_build_with_four_distinct_scope_populations(self) -> None:
 40 |         run = run_with_detection(seed=1, days=5, spec=SCALE_SPECS[2000])
 41 |         reg = run.simulation.registry
 42 |         self.assertEqual(len(run.simulation.agents), 2000)
 43 |         self.assertEqual(reg.campus_population(), 2000)
 44 |         self.assertEqual({s.population for s in reg.scopes.values()}, {2000, 500, 125, 25})
 45 |         self.assertEqual(
 46 |             {s.level for s in reg.scopes.values()},
 47 |             {ScopeLevel.CAMPUS, ScopeLevel.BUILDING, ScopeLevel.FLOOR, ScopeLevel.SUITE},
 48 |         )
 49 | 
 50 |     def test_evidence_gate_matches_its_formula_at_the_largest_size(self) -> None:
 51 |         r = measure_size(2000, list(range(1, 13)))
 52 |         self.assertEqual(r.errors, 0)
 53 |         self.assertGreater(r.scope_evaluations_checked, 0)
 54 |         self.assertEqual(r.threshold_formula_violations, 0)
 55 |         self.assertEqual(r.pass_flag_violations, 0)
 56 |         self.assertEqual(r.selected_without_both_gates, 0)
 57 |         # ceil(sqrt(n)) at every level of the 2,000-agent structure
 58 |         self.assertEqual(
 59 |             r.thresholds_by_level,
 60 |             {
 61 |                 "CAMPUS": max(5, math.isqrt(2000) + 1),   # 45
 62 |                 "BUILDING": max(5, math.isqrt(500) + 1),  # 23
 63 |                 "FLOOR": max(5, math.isqrt(125) + 1),     # 12
 64 |                 "SUITE": 5,
 65 |             },
 66 |         )
 67 | 
 68 |     def test_privacy_gate_still_triggers_at_the_largest_size(self) -> None:
 69 |         r = measure_size(2000, list(range(1, 13)))
 70 |         self.assertGreater(r.roster_refusals, 0)
 71 |         self.assertGreater(r.n_established, 0)

 74 | class RealismOptInTest(unittest.TestCase):
 75 |     def test_default_config_is_the_clean_model(self) -> None:
 76 |         self.assertEqual(DEFAULT_SIMULATION_CONFIG.reporting_model, REPORTING_MODEL_CLEAN)

 80 |             SimulationConfig(reporting_model="whatever")

 84 |         with self.assertRaises(ValueError):
 85 |             simulate_and_report(seed=1, days=3, config=cfg)

 87 |     def test_run_with_detection_dispatches_on_the_config(self) -> None:
 88 |         cfg = replace(DEFAULT_SIMULATION_CONFIG, reporting_model=REPORTING_MODEL_REALISTIC)
 89 |         clean = run_with_detection(seed=4, days=30)
 90 |         realistic = run_with_detection(seed=4, days=30, config=cfg)
 91 |         self.assertNotEqual(_sig(clean), _sig(realistic))
 92 | 
 93 |     def test_all_effects_off_reduces_to_the_clean_model_exactly(self) -> None:
 94 |         off = RealismConfig(heterogeneity=False, correlation=False, stigma=False)
 95 |         for seed in (1, 4, 9):
 96 |             self.assertEqual(
 97 |                 _sig(run_with_detection(seed=seed, days=30)),
 98 |                 _sig(run_realistic(seed=seed, days=30, realism=off)),
 99 |             )

103 |         realistic = run_realistic(seed=7, days=30)
104 |         self.assertEqual(
105 |             [tuple(s.counts.values()) for s in clean.simulation.history],
106 |             [tuple(s.counts.values()) for s in realistic.simulation.history],
107 |         )

110 | class RealismEffectsTest(unittest.TestCase):
111 |     def test_heterogeneity_keeps_the_mean_but_widens_the_spread(self) -> None:
112 |         run = run_realistic(
113 |             seed=3, days=0,
114 |             realism=RealismConfig(heterogeneity=True, correlation=False, stigma=False),
115 |         )
116 |         p = [a.reporting_probability for a in run.simulation.agents]
117 |         self.assertAlmostEqual(sum(p) / len(p), 0.5, delta=0.08)
118 |         self.assertLess(min(p), 0.3)   # the clean model never goes below 0.3
119 |         self.assertGreater(max(p), 0.7)
120 | 
121 |     def test_correlation_raises_total_reports(self) -> None:
122 |         only_corr = RealismConfig(heterogeneity=False, correlation=True, stigma=False)
123 |         base = corr = 0
124 |         for seed in range(1, 21):
125 |             base += len(run_with_detection(seed=seed, days=30).reports)
126 |             corr += len(run_realistic(seed=seed, days=30, realism=only_corr).reports)
127 |         self.assertGreater(corr, base)
128 | 
129 |     def test_stigma_lowers_reports_and_never_changes_the_first_disclosure(self) -> None:
130 |         only_stigma = RealismConfig(heterogeneity=False, correlation=False, stigma=True)
131 |         base = stig = 0
132 |         for seed in range(1, 21):
133 |             a = run_with_detection(seed=seed, days=30)
134 |             b = run_realistic(seed=seed, days=30, realism=only_stigma)
135 |             base += len(a.reports)
136 |             stig += len(b.reports)
137 |             # suppression only starts after a scope is named
138 |             self.assertEqual(
139 |                 a.daily_records[-1].first_disclosed_day,
140 |                 b.daily_records[-1].first_disclosed_day,
141 |             )
142 |         self.assertLess(stig, base)

tests/test_simulation.py

  1 | """Simulation core tests: state machine, contact model, population builder.
  2 | 
  3 | These do not touch the detection or disclosure engines -- they prove the
  4 | simulated world behaves before any Report is generated from it.
  5 | """

 23 | # Derived from the default spec, not hard-coded.
 24 | _SPEC = StructureSpec()
 25 | _TOTAL_AGENTS = (
 26 |     _SPEC.buildings
 27 |     * _SPEC.floors_per_building
 28 |     * _SPEC.suites_per_floor
 29 |     * _SPEC.agents_per_suite

 42 |             self.assertEqual(snapshot.count(InfectionState.SYMPTOMATIC), 0)
 43 |             self.assertEqual(snapshot.count(InfectionState.RECOVERED), 0)

 46 | class CrossSuiteTransmissionTest(unittest.TestCase):
 47 |     """Contact is tiered (suite / floor / building), so an outbreak is no
 48 |     longer trapped in its starting suite -- but the floor/building rates
 49 |     are lower, so crossing a suite boundary should stay rarer than
 50 |     transmitting within one."""
 51 | 
 52 |     def test_cross_suite_transmission_occurs_but_is_rarer_than_within_suite(
 53 |         self,
 54 |     ) -> None:
 55 |         total_within = 0
 56 |         total_cross = 0
 57 |         for seed in range(1, 41):
 58 |             sim = Simulation(seed=seed, seed_infections=1)
 59 |             sim.run(30)
 60 |             total_within += sim.total_within_suite_infections
 61 |             total_cross += sim.total_cross_suite_infections
 62 | 
 63 |         self.assertGreater(
 64 |             total_cross, 0, "cross-suite transmission never occurred over 40 seeds"
 65 |         )
 66 |         self.assertLess(
 67 |             total_cross,
 68 |             total_within,
 69 |             f"cross-suite transmission ({total_cross}) was not rarer than "
 70 |             f"within-suite transmission ({total_within})",
 71 |         )
 72 | 
 73 | 
 74 | class WithinSuiteAttackRateTest(unittest.TestCase):
 75 |     """A seeded suite's final attack rate should be a real, seed-dependent
 76 |     outcome -- never a guaranteed 100%, and not the same number every
 77 |     time."""
 78 | 
 79 |     def _suite_attack_rate(self, seed: int, days: int = 30) -> float:
 80 |         sim = Simulation(seed=seed, seed_infections=1)
 81 |         seeded_suite = next(
 82 |             a.suite_id for a in sim.agents if a.state is not InfectionState.SUSCEPTIBLE
 83 |         )
 84 |         history = sim.run(days)
 85 |         last = history[-1]
 86 |         n = sum(1 for s in last.agents if s.suite_id == seeded_suite)
 87 |         infected = sum(
 88 |             1
 89 |             for s in last.agents
 90 |             if s.suite_id == seeded_suite and s.state is not InfectionState.SUSCEPTIBLE
 91 |         )
 92 |         return infected / n
 93 | 
 94 |     def test_attack_rate_is_below_100_percent_and_varies_across_seeds(self) -> None:
 95 |         rates = [self._suite_attack_rate(seed) for seed in range(1, 11)]
 96 | 
 97 |         self.assertTrue(
 98 |             all(rate < 1.0 for rate in rates),
 99 |             f"some seed reached a 100% within-suite attack rate: {rates}",
100 |         )
101 |         self.assertGreater(
102 |             max(rates) - min(rates),
103 |             0.1,
104 |             f"within-suite attack rate did not vary meaningfully across seeds: {rates}",
105 |         )
106 |

108 | class SecondFloorReachedTest(unittest.TestCase):
109 |     def test_infection_reaches_a_second_floor_at_least_sometimes(self) -> None:
110 |         reached_second_floor = False
111 |         for seed in range(1, 31):
112 |             sim = Simulation(seed=seed, seed_infections=1)
113 |             history = sim.run(30)
114 | 
115 |             touched_floors = {
116 |                 suite_ancestor_ids(sim.registry, s.suite_id)[0]
117 |                 for s in history[-1].agents
118 |                 if s.state is not InfectionState.SUSCEPTIBLE
119 |             }
120 |             if len(touched_floors) > 1:
121 |                 reached_second_floor = True
122 |                 break
123 | 
124 |         self.assertTrue(
125 |             reached_second_floor,
126 |             "no seed among the first 30 reached a second floor within 30 days",
127 |         )

130 | class CertainTransmissionTest(unittest.TestCase):
131 |     def test_probability_one_infects_the_whole_suite_in_a_single_step(self) -> None:
132 |         # With p = 1.0 a single infectious agent infects every susceptible
133 |         # suite-mate in one daily step, so the expected number of steps to
134 |         # a fully-infected suite is 1.
135 |         config = SimulationConfig(suite_transmission_probability=1.0)
136 |         sim = Simulation(seed=3, seed_infections=1, config=config)
137 |         seeded_suite = next(
138 |             a.suite_id for a in sim.agents if a.state is not InfectionState.SUSCEPTIBLE
139 |         )
140 | 
141 |         history = sim.run(5)
142 |         day_one = history[1]
143 |         in_suite = [s for s in day_one.agents if s.suite_id == seeded_suite]
144 | 
145 |         self.assertEqual(len(in_suite), _SPEC.agents_per_suite)
146 |         self.assertTrue(
147 |             all(s.state is not InfectionState.SUSCEPTIBLE for s in in_suite)

151 | class ForwardProgressionTest(unittest.TestCase):
152 |     def test_states_never_regress_and_never_skip(self) -> None:
153 |         config = SimulationConfig(suite_transmission_probability=1.0)
154 |         sim = Simulation(seed=5, seed_infections=1, config=config)
155 |         history = sim.run(30)
156 | 
157 |         sequences: dict[str, list[InfectionState]] = {}
158 |         for snapshot in history:
159 |             for agent_snapshot in snapshot.agents:
160 |                 sequences.setdefault(agent_snapshot.id, []).append(agent_snapshot.state)
161 | 
162 |         for agent_id, states in sequences.items():
163 |             for previous, following in zip(states, states[1:]):
164 |                 delta = order_index(following) - order_index(previous)
165 |                 self.assertGreaterEqual(delta, 0, f"{agent_id} moved backwards")
166 |                 self.assertLessEqual(delta, 1, f"{agent_id} skipped a state")
167 | 
168 |         # Non-vacuous: the p=1.0 suite runs the full course within 30 days.
169 |         self.assertGreater(history[-1].count(InfectionState.RECOVERED), 0)
170 |

172 | class ConservationTest(unittest.TestCase):
173 |     def test_agent_count_is_conserved_across_states_every_step(self) -> None:
174 |         config = SimulationConfig(suite_transmission_probability=0.5)
175 |         sim = Simulation(seed=2, seed_infections=2, config=config)
176 |         history = sim.run(30)
177 | 
178 |         total = len(sim.agents)
179 |         for snapshot in history:
180 |             self.assertEqual(sum(snapshot.counts.values()), total)
181 |             self.assertEqual(len(snapshot.agents), total)
182 |             self.assertEqual(set(snapshot.counts), set(STATE_ORDER))

185 | class RegistryPopulationTest(unittest.TestCase):
186 |     def setUp(self) -> None:
187 |         self.spec = StructureSpec()
188 |         self.agents, self.registry = build_population(self.spec, random.Random(0))

190 |     def test_declared_populations_equal_actual_agent_counts(self) -> None:
191 |         for scope_id, scope in self.registry.scopes.items():
192 |             actual = sum(
193 |                 1
194 |                 for agent in self.agents
195 |                 if self.registry.contains(scope_id, agent.suite_id)
196 |             )
197 |             self.assertEqual(scope.population, actual, f"scope {scope_id}")

199 |     def test_populations_per_level_match_the_spec(self) -> None:
200 |         expected_suite = self.spec.agents_per_suite
201 |         expected_floor = self.spec.suites_per_floor * expected_suite
202 |         expected_campus = _TOTAL_AGENTS
203 |

205 |         for scope in self.registry.scopes.values():
206 |             per_level.setdefault(scope.level, set()).add(scope.population)
207 | 
208 |         self.assertEqual(per_level[ScopeLevel.SUITE], {expected_suite})
209 |         self.assertEqual(per_level[ScopeLevel.FLOOR], {expected_floor})
210 |         self.assertEqual(per_level[ScopeLevel.CAMPUS], {expected_campus})
211 |         self.assertEqual(len(self.agents), expected_campus)
212 |         # The concrete acceptance numbers from the task.
213 |         self.assertEqual(expected_suite, 25)
214 |         self.assertEqual(expected_floor, 75)
215 |         self.assertEqual(expected_campus, 150)

248 | class DeterminismTest(unittest.TestCase):
249 |     def test_same_seed_same_history(self) -> None:
250 |         a = Simulation(seed=42, seed_infections=1).run(20)
251 |         b = Simulation(seed=42, seed_infections=1).run(20)
252 |         self.assertEqual(
253 |             [s.counts for s in a],
254 |             [s.counts for s in b],
255 |         )
256 | 
257 | 
258 | def _force_state(agent: Agent, target: InfectionState) -> None:
259 |     while order_index(agent.state) < order_index(target):
260 |         infection.set_state(agent, STATE_ORDER[order_index(agent.state) + 1], 0)
261 | 
262 | 
263 | def _curve(seed: int, days: int) -> tuple[list[int], list[int]]:
264 |     history = Simulation(seed=seed, seed_infections=1).run(days)
265 |     active = [
266 |         h.count(InfectionState.INCUBATING) + h.count(InfectionState.SYMPTOMATIC)
267 |         for h in history
268 |     ]
269 |     cumulative = [
270 |         sum(1 for a in h.agents if a.state is not InfectionState.SUSCEPTIBLE)
271 |         for h in history
272 |     ]
273 |     return active, cumulative
274 | 
275 | 
276 | class EpidemicCurvePlausibilityTest(unittest.TestCase):
277 |     """Sept 7 milestone: across seeds the headless simulator produces
278 |     believable outbreaks -- a rise, a peak, a decline -- and is neither
279 |     always-zero nor always-everyone."""
280 | 
281 |     DAYS = 30
282 |     SEEDS = tuple(range(1, 41))
283 |     ESTABLISHED = 10  # cumulative infections that count as a real outbreak
284 | 
285 |     @classmethod
286 |     def setUpClass(cls) -> None:
287 |         cls.runs = [(s, *_curve(s, cls.DAYS)) for s in cls.SEEDS]
288 |         cls.total = len(Simulation(seed=1).agents)
289 | 
290 |     def _established(self):
291 |         return [(s, a, c) for s, a, c in self.runs if c[-1] >= self.ESTABLISHED]
292 | 
293 |     def test_no_seed_infects_the_whole_population(self) -> None:
294 |         for seed, _active, cum in self.runs:
295 |             self.assertLess(cum[-1], self.total, f"seed {seed} infected everyone")
296 | 
297 |     def test_some_seeds_fizzle_and_some_take_off(self) -> None:
298 |         established = self._established()
299 |         fizzled = [r for r in self.runs if r[2][-1] <= 3]
300 |         self.assertGreater(len(established), 5, "almost no seed produced an outbreak")
301 |         self.assertLess(
302 |             len(established), len(self.runs),
303 |             "every seed produced an outbreak; the branching process is degenerate",
304 |         )
305 |         self.assertGreater(len(fizzled), 0, "no seed ever fizzled from its index case")
306 | 
307 |     def test_a_real_outbreak_reaches_a_large_share_of_the_population(self) -> None:
308 |         worst = max(cum[-1] for _s, _a, cum in self.runs)
309 |         self.assertGreater(worst, self.total * 0.4)
310 | 
311 |     def test_cumulative_infections_never_decrease(self) -> None:
312 |         for seed, _active, cum in self.runs:
313 |             for earlier, later in zip(cum, cum[1:]):
314 |                 self.assertLessEqual(earlier, later, f"seed {seed} cumulative fell")
315 | 
316 |     def test_established_outbreaks_rise_off_day_zero_and_mostly_turn_over(self) -> None:
317 |         established = self._established()
318 |         self.assertTrue(established)
319 |         turned_over = 0
320 |         for seed, active, _cum in established:
321 |             peak = max(active)
322 |             peak_day = active.index(peak)
323 |             self.assertGreater(peak, active[0], f"seed {seed} never rose")
324 |             self.assertGreaterEqual(peak_day, 2, f"seed {seed} peaked at day {peak_day}")
325 |             if peak_day <= self.DAYS - 3 and active[-1] < peak:
326 |                 turned_over += 1
327 |         self.assertGreaterEqual(
328 |             turned_over, 0.6 * len(established),
329 |             "most established outbreaks did not peak and decline inside the window",
330 |         )
331 | 
332 |     def test_a_long_run_burns_out_to_zero_active(self) -> None:
333 |         seed = self._established()[0][0]
334 |         active, _cum = _curve(seed, 90)
335 |         self.assertEqual(active[-1], 0, f"seed {seed} still active at day 90")

391 |         spec = StructureSpec(

scripts/precompute_results.py

  1 | """Run the three heavy analyses once and cache their outputs as JSON.
  2 | 
  3 |     python -m scripts.precompute_results
  4 | 
  5 | Writes results/disclosure_sweep.json, results/benchmark.json and
  6 | results/adversarial.json. Those files are committed and served by the API
  7 | so nothing heavy runs on request. Re-run this when the underlying policy
  8 | constants or the model change.
  9 | """
 10 | 
 11 | from __future__ import annotations
 12 | 
 13 | import json
 14 | import sys
 15 | import time
 16 | from pathlib import Path
 17 | 
 18 | sys.path.insert(0, str(Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent))
 19 | 
 20 | from adversarial.selftest import run_self_test  # noqa: E402
 21 | from simulation.benchmark import (  # noqa: E402
 22 |     DayRow,
 23 |     benchmark_cross_regime,
 24 |     benchmark_in_distribution,
 25 |     split_by_run,
 26 | )
 27 | from simulation.config import (  # noqa: E402
 28 |     BENCHMARK_DEFAULT_SEEDS,
 29 |     BENCHMARK_TEST_SUITE_TRANSMISSIONS,
 30 |     DEFAULT_EXPERIMENT_SEEDS,
 31 |     DEFAULT_RUN_DAYS,
 32 | )
 33 | from simulation.experiments import run_disclosure_sweeps  # noqa: E402
 34 | 
 35 | RESULTS_DIR = Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent / "results"
 36 | 
 37 | ADVERSARIAL_SEEDS = 200
 38 | 
 39 | 
 40 | def _write(name: str, payload: dict) -> None:
 41 |     RESULTS_DIR.mkdir(exist_ok=True)
 42 |     path = RESULTS_DIR / f"{name}.json"
 43 |     path.write_text(json.dumps(payload, indent=2) + "\n")
 44 |     print(f"  wrote {path.relative_to(RESULTS_DIR.parent)}")
 45 | 
 46 | 
 47 | def build_disclosure_sweep() -> dict:
 48 |     results = run_disclosure_sweeps()
 49 |     sweeps: dict[str, dict] = {}
 50 |     for name, summaries in results.sweeps.items():
 51 |         rows = []
 52 |         for s in summaries:
 53 |             rows.append(
 54 |                 {
 55 |                     "value": s.value,
 56 |                     "degenerate": s.degenerate,
 57 |                     "degenerate_reason": s.degenerate_reason,
 58 |                     "n_disclosed": s.n_disclosed,
 59 |                     "n_established": s.n_established,
 60 |                     "disc_delay": s.mean_disclosure_delay,
 61 |                     "fire_delay": s.mean_fire_delay,
 62 |                     "gap": s.mean_gap,
 63 |                     "inf_before_disclosure_unbiased": s.mean_infections_before_disclosure_unbiased,
 64 |                     "inf_at_disclosure_conditional": s.mean_infections_at_disclosure,
 65 |                     "finest_level": (
 66 |                         s.finest_disclosed_level.name if s.finest_disclosed_level else None
 67 |                     ),
 68 |                 }
 69 |             )
 70 |         sweeps[name] = {"rows": rows}
 71 |     return {
 72 |         "seeds": DEFAULT_EXPERIMENT_SEEDS,
 73 |         "days": DEFAULT_RUN_DAYS,
 74 |         "fizzle_rate": results.fizzle_rate,
 75 |         "false_alarm_rate": results.false_alarm_rate,
 76 |         "control_fire_delay": results.control_fire_delay,
 77 |         "primary_sweep": "STATISTICAL_GATE_FLOOR",
 78 |         "sweeps": sweeps,
 79 |     }
 80 | 
 81 | 
 82 | def _score_dict(score) -> dict:
 83 |     return {
 84 |         "precision": round(score.precision, 3),
 85 |         "recall": round(score.recall, 3),
 86 |         "delay": (
 87 |             None if score.mean_detection_delay is None else round(score.mean_detection_delay, 2)
 88 |         ),
 89 |     }
 90 | 
 91 | 
 92 | def build_benchmark() -> dict:
 93 |     all_seeds = list(range(1, BENCHMARK_DEFAULT_SEEDS + 1))
 94 |     in_dist = benchmark_in_distribution(all_seeds)
 95 |     train_seeds, test_seeds = split_by_run(
 96 |         [DayRow(s, 0, 0.0, 0, 0, 0, False) for s in all_seeds]
 97 |     )
 98 |     train_ids = sorted({r.seed for r in train_seeds})
 99 |     test_ids = sorted({r.seed for r in test_seeds})
100 |     cross = benchmark_cross_regime(train_ids, test_ids)
101 | 
102 |     cross_regime = []
103 |     model_still_wins = True
104 |     for res, tp in zip(cross, BENCHMARK_TEST_SUITE_TRANSMISSIONS):
105 |         rules, model = res.scores[0], res.scores[1]
106 |         cross_regime.append(
107 |             {
108 |                 "label": f"suite p={tp:g}",
109 |                 "rules": _score_dict(rules),
110 |                 "model": _score_dict(model),
111 |             }
112 |         )
113 |         if not (model.recall > rules.recall + 0.02):
114 |             model_still_wins = False
115 | 
116 |     if model_still_wins:
117 |         cross_conclusion = (
118 |             "The model's small recall and latency edge survives the shift in "
119 |             "transmission probability: it is not purely memorised. The edge is still "
120 |             "small and still bought with lower precision, and it is still a model of "
121 |             "this simulator."
122 |         )
123 |     else:
124 |         cross_conclusion = (
125 |             "The model's advantage does not hold once the transmission probability "
126 |             "differs from training. Reported as a negative result: the classifier "
127 |             "mostly learned this simulator."
128 |         )
129 | 
130 |     return {
131 |         "seeds": BENCHMARK_DEFAULT_SEEDS,
132 |         "days": DEFAULT_RUN_DAYS,
133 |         "in_distribution": {
134 |             "rules": _score_dict(in_dist.scores[0]),
135 |             "model": _score_dict(in_dist.scores[1]),
136 |         },
137 |         "conclusion": (
138 |             "In distribution the learned model is marginally faster and catches a few "
139 |             "more real outbreak-days, at a cost of about three points of precision on a "
140 |             "detector that already over-alarms. The authored threshold rules ship; the "
141 |             "model is kept only as this benchmark."
142 |         ),
143 |         "cross_regime": cross_regime,
144 |         "cross_regime_conclusion": cross_conclusion,
145 |     }
146 | 
147 | 
148 | def build_adversarial() -> dict:
149 |     s = run_self_test(n_seeds=ADVERSARIAL_SEEDS)
150 |     return {
151 |         "seeds": s.n_runs,
152 |         "days": s.days,
153 |         "same_scope_pairs": s.same_scope_pairs,
154 |         "exact_one_person_pins": s.exact_one_person_pinned,
155 |         "band_one_person_pins": s.band_one_person_pinned,
156 |         "exact_direction_known": s.exact_direction_known,
157 |         "band_direction_known": s.band_direction_known,
158 |         "band_median_possible_deltas": (
159 |             None
160 |             if s.band_median_possible_values == float("inf")
161 |             else round(s.band_median_possible_values)
162 |         ),
163 |         "wandering_on_mean": round(s.wandering_on_mean, 2),
164 |         "wandering_off_mean": round(s.wandering_off_mean, 2),
165 |         "wandering_on_max": s.wandering_on_max,
166 |         "wandering_off_max": s.wandering_off_max,
167 |         "residual": s.band_residual,
168 |     }
169 | 
170 | 
171 | def main() -> None:
172 |     steps = (
173 |         ("disclosure_sweep", build_disclosure_sweep),
174 |         ("benchmark", build_benchmark),
175 |         ("adversarial", build_adversarial),
176 |     )
177 |     for name, build in steps:
178 |         start = time.time()
179 |         print(f"{name} ...", flush=True)
180 |         _write(name, build())
181 |         print(f"  {time.time() - start:.0f}s")
182 | 
183 | 
184 | if __name__ == "__main__":
185 |     main()

scripts/stress_tests.py

 1 | """Run the two limitation-closing stress tests and cache them as JSON.
 2 | 
 3 |     python -m scripts.stress_tests             # both (a few minutes)
 4 |     python -m scripts.stress_tests --scale     # scale validation only
 5 |     python -m scripts.stress_tests --realism   # reporting-realism only
 6 | 
 7 | Writes results/scale.json and results/reporting_realism.json. They are
 8 | committed for the docs to cite; the API does not serve them, and
 9 | ``scripts.precompute_results`` (the three shipped panels) is unchanged.
10 | """
11 | 
12 | from __future__ import annotations
13 | 
14 | import argparse
15 | import json
16 | import sys
17 | import time
18 | from dataclasses import asdict
19 | from pathlib import Path
20 | 
21 | sys.path.insert(0, str(Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent))
22 | 
23 | from simulation.realism import (  # noqa: E402
24 |     VARIANTS,
25 |     format_sweep,
26 |     run_realism_sweep,
27 |     run_sensitivity_grid,
28 | )
29 | from simulation.scale import format_reports, run_scale_validation  # noqa: E402
30 | 
31 | RESULTS_DIR = Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent / "results"
32 | SEEDS = list(range(1, 501))
33 | 
34 | 
35 | def _write(name: str, payload: dict) -> None:
36 |     RESULTS_DIR.mkdir(exist_ok=True)
37 |     path = RESULTS_DIR / f"{name}.json"
38 |     path.write_text(json.dumps(payload, indent=2) + "\n")
39 |     print(f"  wrote {path.relative_to(RESULTS_DIR.parent)}")
40 | 
41 | 
42 | def build_scale() -> dict:
43 |     reports = run_scale_validation(SEEDS)
44 |     print(format_reports(reports))
45 |     return {"seeds": len(SEEDS), "days": 30, "sizes": [asdict(r) for r in reports]}
46 | 
47 | 
48 | def build_realism() -> dict:
49 |     sweep = run_realism_sweep(SEEDS)
50 |     print(format_sweep(sweep))
51 |     grid = run_sensitivity_grid(SEEDS)
52 |     return {"seeds": len(SEEDS), "days": 30, "variants": sweep, "grid": grid,
53 |             "variant_names": list(VARIANTS)}
54 | 
55 | 
56 | def main() -> None:
57 |     parser = argparse.ArgumentParser()
58 |     parser.add_argument("--scale", action="store_true")
59 |     parser.add_argument("--realism", action="store_true")
60 |     args = parser.parse_args()
61 |     both = not (args.scale or args.realism)
62 |     if args.scale or both:
63 |         t = time.time()
64 |         _write("scale", build_scale())
65 |         print(f"  {time.time() - t:.0f}s")
66 |     if args.realism or both:
67 |         t = time.time()
68 |         _write("reporting_realism", build_realism())
69 |         print(f"  {time.time() - t:.0f}s")
70 | 
71 | 
72 | if __name__ == "__main__":
73 |     main()

scripts/verify_epidemic_curves.py

 1 | """Week 1 sanity check: do the simulator's epidemic curves look plausible?
 2 | 
 3 |     python -m scripts.verify_epidemic_curves
 4 | 
 5 | Runs the headless simulator across a range of seeds and prints, per seed,
 6 | the peak active count, the day it peaks, the final attack rate, and the
 7 | curve shape. Then a PASS/FAIL summary: not always-zero, not
 8 | always-everyone, most established outbreaks rise then turn over. This is a
 9 | milestone record, not a test -- the assertions live in
10 | tests/test_simulation.py::EpidemicCurvePlausibilityTest.
11 | """
12 | 
13 | from __future__ import annotations
14 | 
15 | import sys
16 | from pathlib import Path
17 | 
18 | sys.path.insert(0, str(Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent))
19 | 
20 | from simulation.infection import InfectionState  # noqa: E402
21 | from simulation.simulator import Simulation  # noqa: E402
22 | 
23 | SEEDS = range(1, 41)
24 | DAYS = 30
25 | ESTABLISHED = 10
26 | 
27 | 
28 | def main() -> None:
29 |     total = len(Simulation(seed=1).agents)
30 |     established = fizzled = turned_over = 0
31 |     worst_attack = 0.0
32 | 
33 |     print(f"{total} agents, {DAYS} days, seeds {SEEDS.start}..{SEEDS.stop - 1}")
34 |     print(f"{'seed':>4} {'peak':>5} {'peak_day':>8} {'attack%':>8}  shape")
35 |     for seed in SEEDS:
36 |         history = Simulation(seed=seed, seed_infections=1).run(DAYS)
37 |         active = [
38 |             h.count(InfectionState.INCUBATING) + h.count(InfectionState.SYMPTOMATIC)
39 |             for h in history
40 |         ]
41 |         cumulative = sum(
42 |             1 for a in history[-1].agents if a.state is not InfectionState.SUSCEPTIBLE
43 |         )
44 |         peak = max(active)
45 |         peak_day = active.index(peak)
46 |         attack = cumulative / total * 100
47 |         worst_attack = max(worst_attack, attack)
48 | 
49 |         shape = []
50 |         if cumulative >= ESTABLISHED:
51 |             established += 1
52 |             if peak > active[0]:
53 |                 shape.append("rise")
54 |             if peak_day <= DAYS - 3 and active[-1] < peak:
55 |                 shape.append("decline")
56 |                 turned_over += 1
57 |         elif cumulative <= 3:
58 |             fizzled += 1
59 |             shape.append("fizzle")
60 |         print(f"{seed:>4} {peak:>5} {peak_day:>8} {attack:>7.1f}  {'/'.join(shape)}")
61 | 
62 |     n = len(list(SEEDS))
63 |     checks = [
64 |         (established > 5, f"established outbreaks: {established}/{n} (> 5)"),
65 |         (established < n, f"non-degenerate: {n - established}/{n} did not take off"),
66 |         (fizzled > 0, f"index-case fizzle happens: {fizzled}/{n}"),
67 |         (worst_attack > 40.0, f"a real outbreak occurs: worst attack {worst_attack:.0f}% (> 40)"),
68 |         (worst_attack < 100.0, f"never everyone: worst attack {worst_attack:.0f}% (< 100)"),
69 |         (
70 |             turned_over >= 0.6 * established,
71 |             f"most established outbreaks peak then decline: {turned_over}/{established}",
72 |         ),
73 |     ]
74 |     print()
75 |     ok = True
76 |     for passed, label in checks:
77 |         print(f"  [{'PASS' if passed else 'FAIL'}] {label}")
78 |         ok = ok and passed
79 |     print()
80 |     print("RESULT:", "believable outbreaks" if ok else "SOMETHING IS OFF")
81 |     sys.exit(0 if ok else 1)
82 | 
83 | 
84 | if __name__ == "__main__":
85 |     main()

CLAUDE.md

  1 | # MicroCluster
  2 | 
  3 | **Thesis: it detects the signal without exposing the person who created it.**
  4 | 
  5 | MicroCluster is a privacy-preserving health-signal system for small shared
  6 | communities (a co-op, a dorm, an office floor, a shared studio building).
  7 | People submit anonymous symptom reports from a **fixed checklist**. The
  8 | system looks for statistically unusual activity and, when it finds some,
  9 | discloses it **only at a scope that is both statistically supported and
 10 | privacy-safe** — otherwise it discloses nothing.
 11 | 
 12 | This is not an outbreak-prediction tool and it does not diagnose anyone.
 13 | The engineering problem is narrow and specific:
 14 | 
 15 | > Extract a community-level signal from sparse, anonymous data without
 16 | > letting the signal become a privacy leak.
 17 | 
 18 | **Idea locked September 2. No concept evaluation before October 6.**
 19 | 
 20 | Everything below is a **decided rule**, not a design open for revision.
 21 | 
 22 | ---
 23 | 
 24 | ## What is in this repository right now
 25 | 
 26 | Only three things:
 27 | 
 28 | 1. **Detection engine** (`microcluster/detection.py`) — decides whether
 29 |    anything unusual is happening.
 30 | 2. **Disclosure engine** (`microcluster/disclosure.py`) — decides what, if
 31 |    anything, may be said out loud and at what scope.
 32 | 3. **Tests** (`tests/`) — cover the scenarios listed at the bottom.
 33 | 
 34 | No frontend. No API. No replay mode. `microcluster/engine.py` is a thin
 35 | wire between the two engines and `microcluster/demo.py` prints the six
 36 | reference scenarios.
 37 | 
 38 | Detection and disclosure are **separate, independently testable modules**.
 39 | They share only plain aggregate data structures. The disclosure engine
 40 | never receives individual records (see rule 10).
 41 | 
 42 | **Being added now (see the SIMULATION section):** an interactive simulator
 43 | that generates anonymous reports and feeds them to the detection engine to
 44 | measure how late the detector catches an outbreak. It does **not** modify
 45 | `config.py`, `models.py`, `detection.py`, `disclosure.py`, `engine.py` or
 46 | their tests — those are the foundation and stay exactly as they are. The
 47 | simulator only produces `Report` objects for the existing engines to
 48 | consume.
 49 | 
 50 | ---
 51 | 
 52 | ## Decided rules
 53 | 
 54 | ### 1. Fixed checklist, never free text
 55 | 
 56 | Input is a **fixed checklist**. There is no natural-language interpretation
 57 | anywhere in the system.
 58 | 
 59 | - Four categories: **Respiratory, Gastrointestinal, General, Other**, each
 60 |   with 3–5 named symptoms (`microcluster/models.py::CHECKLIST`).
 61 | - Coarse onset: `today` / `1-2 days` / `3-7 days` / `1+ week`.
 62 | - A location group: one node in the scope hierarchy.
 63 | 
 64 | The specific symptom is recorded for the reporter's own clarity. It is
 65 | **never** used for matching.
 66 | 
 67 | ### 2. Clustering at CATEGORY level only
 68 | 
 69 | Reports are only ever grouped by **category**, never by individual symptom.
 70 | This is what makes *"we don't diagnose"* structurally true rather than a
 71 | promise — the code has no finer-grained medical concept to reason about.
 72 | 
 73 | ### 3. Two independent detectors, either can fire
 74 | 
 75 | - **Relative detector** — is this location unusual compared to its
 76 |   *contemporaneous peers* (sibling scopes, same detection window)?
 77 | - **Absolute detector** — is overall activity unusual compared to a
 78 |   configured background rate?
 79 | 
 80 | Either one firing counts as detection. They are computed separately and
 81 | reported separately.
 82 | 
 83 | ### 4. `BACKGROUND_RATE` is a named, configurable, illustrative constant
 84 | 
 85 | `microcluster/config.py::BACKGROUND_RATE` is the approximate fraction of a
 86 | population expected to file a qualifying report within one detection
 87 | window under ordinary conditions.
 88 | 
 89 | **It is an illustrative figure, not a clinical or epidemiological
 90 | estimate.** It lives in `config.py` with a comment saying exactly that.
 91 | It is never inlined into scoring code. Tune it from real baseline data
 92 | before trusting the absolute detector.
 93 | 
 94 | ### 5. `DETECTION_WINDOW` is a named constant, default 72 hours
 95 | 
 96 | `microcluster/config.py::DETECTION_WINDOW_HOURS = 72`. A report is
 97 | "current activity" if it was submitted within this many hours of the
 98 | evaluation time.
 99 | 
100 | ### 6. No composite score
101 | 
102 | The detection engine reports **three findings, plainly**, and never
103 | combines them into one number:
104 | 
105 | - `relative_excess_ratio` — the driving location's per-capita rate divided
106 |   by its peers' pooled per-capita rate.
107 | - `count_within_window` — total qualifying reports in the detection window.
108 | - `count_sharing_category` — how many of those share a single category.
109 | 
110 | A composite score would hide which of these actually moved, and would
111 | invite threshold-tuning that trades privacy for sensitivity invisibly.
112 | 
113 | ### 7. Every scope has a DECLARED population `n`
114 | 
115 | Each scope (campus / building / floor / suite) carries a population `n`
116 | **declared at group creation**. It is never the number of people who
117 | happened to report. All gate math uses the declared `n`.
118 | 
119 | ### 8. Two separate gates, both must pass
120 | 
121 | A scope may be disclosed only if it passes **both** of these, evaluated
122 | independently and reported separately:
123 | 
124 | - **Statistical gate:**
125 |   `qualifying_reports >= max(STATISTICAL_GATE_FLOOR, ceil(sqrt(n)))`
126 |   (`STATISTICAL_GATE_FLOOR = 5`). This scales **up** with `n`: a claim
127 |   about a bigger population needs more evidence.
128 | 
129 | - **Privacy gate:**
130 |   `n >= MIN_SCOPE_POPULATION` (default 20)
131 |   **and** `qualifying_reports / n <= MAX_REPORT_FRACTION` (configurable).
132 |   This scales **down** with `n`: small scopes are the dangerous ones.
133 | 
134 | The two gates **pull in opposite directions by design**. They are never
135 | merged into a single number. Merging them would make the most identifying
136 | statement (a tiny, precise scope) the *cheapest* to make — which is
137 | backwards. Keeping them separate means a small scope must clear a bar that
138 | a large scope does not, and a large scope must clear a bar that a small
139 | scope does not.
140 | 
141 | ### 9. Disclose the finest scope passing both gates
142 | 
143 | Evaluate every candidate scope with the single rule in rule 8. Disclose
144 | the **finest** scope (deepest level, then smallest `n`) that passes both
145 | gates. If none passes, **disclose nothing**. There is no hardcoded tier
146 | list — `campus → building → floor → suite` falls out of applying one rule
147 | to a set of candidates.
148 | 
149 | ### 10. Privacy evaluation runs AFTER detection, before presentation
150 | 
151 | Order is fixed: detection → disclosure → presentation. The disclosure
152 | engine is handed **aggregate per-scope counts and declared populations**,
153 | never individual records. The presentation layer (not built yet) will be
154 | handed only the evaluation table from rule 12 and must render it as-is.
155 | 
156 | ### 11. Anonymity over identity-based abuse prevention
157 | 
158 | Reports are anonymous. That means abuse — someone spamming reports to
159 | manufacture a signal — is **possible, and we accept it** as the cost of
160 | anonymity.
161 | 
162 | Light mitigation only: a per-session submission cap
163 | (`SESSION_SUBMISSION_CAP`) and a per-day cap (`DAILY_SUBMISSION_CAP`),
164 | both named constants in `config.py`, enforced at intake (intake is not
165 | part of this repo yet). We deliberately choose these weak, anonymous
166 | mitigations over any identity-based control (accounts, device binding,
167 | verification), because identity is precisely what the system exists to
168 | protect.
169 | 
170 | ### 12. Every candidate scope gets a complete evaluation record
171 | 
172 | `disclosure.evaluate(...)` returns a `ScopeEvaluation` for **every**
173 | candidate scope, including the rejected ones — not just the winner. Each
174 | record contains:
175 | 
176 | - scope id, label, level
177 | - declared population `n`
178 | - qualifying report count
179 | - statistical threshold and PASS/FAIL
180 | - privacy threshold(s) and PASS/FAIL (minimum population, report fraction)
181 | - final `disclosure_eligible` (both gates) and `selected`
182 | - a `disclosure_reason` string explaining the outcome, e.g.
183 |   *"Rejected: population n=18 below minimum privacy scope (20)"*
184 | 
185 | The winning disclosure is **selected from this table**, never computed on
186 | its own path. A future UI renders these records directly and must never
187 | recompute a gate result itself.
188 | 
189 | ---
190 | 
191 | ## Every threshold is a policy, not a law
192 | 
193 | `ceil(sqrt(n))`, `STATISTICAL_GATE_FLOOR`, `MIN_SCOPE_POPULATION`,
194 | `MAX_REPORT_FRACTION`, `BACKGROUND_RATE`, `ABSOLUTE_EXCESS_RATIO`,
195 | `RELATIVE_EXCESS_RATIO`, `DETECTION_WINDOW_HOURS` — these are
196 | **project-defined policies**. They are:
197 | 
198 | - **named** — all in `microcluster/config.py`,
199 | - **configurable** — via `DetectionConfig` / `DisclosureConfig`,
200 | - **documented as choices** — here and in `config.py` comments.
201 | 
202 | None of them is derived from a proven result. `sqrt(n)` is a reasonable
203 | shape for "evidence should scale with the size of the claim", not a
204 | theorem about this problem.
205 | 
206 | ---
207 | 
208 | ## Detection hysteresis and disclosure scope stability
209 | 
210 | `detection.evaluate` and `disclosure.evaluate` are each still, in
211 | isolation, a pure single-instant computation. Called in a SEQUENCE (once a
212 | day over a live event), both accept optional state threaded from the
213 | previous call, and both use that state the same way: prefer continuity
214 | over reacting to a single noisy instant.
215 | 
216 | - **Detection hysteresis.** Once fired, `fired` is held `True` for at
217 |   least `HYSTERESIS_MIN_FIRED_DAYS` consecutive evaluations even if that
218 |   day's raw relative/absolute check alone says no — an ordinary wobble in
219 |   which reports fall inside a rolling 72h window should not flip
220 |   detection off and back on inside one ongoing event. It releases early
221 |   regardless of the minimum if the window's qualifying count drops to
222 |   `HYSTERESIS_RELEASE_THRESHOLD` or below: a drop that low means the event
223 |   is genuinely over. State is `HysteresisState`, threaded explicitly
224 |   (never global); a call with no state behaves exactly as if hysteresis
225 |   did not exist.
226 | 
227 | - **Disclosure scope stability.** Once a scope has been disclosed, passing
228 |   its id as `prior_disclosed_scope_id` makes `evaluate` prefer to keep
229 |   disclosing that scope, or a coarser ancestor of it if the exact scope
230 |   stops qualifying, over hopping to a different scope. It only switches
231 |   away from that lineage when some other scope's qualifying count exceeds
232 |   it by more than `SIBLING_SWITCH_MARGIN`. Each disclosure still passes
233 |   both gates on its own; the point is that the SEQUENCE of disclosures
234 |   leaks more than any one of them does — an observer who watches it
235 |   wander learns about every group it ever names, not just one. A call
236 |   with no prior id is unaffected.
237 | 
238 | Both constants live in `microcluster/config.py` next to every other
239 | threshold, for the same reason: named, configurable, and not a proven
240 | law. `simulation/pipeline.py` is the reference caller — it threads both
241 | kinds of state day to day and records, in `DailyRecord`, the running
242 | `first_fired_day` and `first_disclosed_day`. The gap between them is the
243 | measurable cost of the privacy policy: how long the system knew something
244 | before it was permitted to say where.
245 | 
246 | ---
247 | 
248 | ## Test scenarios (`tests/`)
249 | 
250 | - **Localized cluster** — relative detector fires, absolute does not; a
251 |   suite-level disclosure.
252 | - **Campus-wide rise** — absolute detector fires, relative does not; a
253 |   campus-level disclosure.
254 | - **Scattered noise** — neither detector fires; nothing disclosed.
255 | - **Overclaim refusal** — 7 reports, campus `n=2000`: statistical gate
256 |   fails (`7 < 45`); no campus-level disclosure.
257 | - **Privacy refusal** — 5 reports, floor `n=18`: statistical gate passes
258 |   (`5 >= 5`) but privacy gate fails (`18 < 20`); a coarser scope is
259 |   disclosed instead.
260 | - **Both gates across populations** — `n ∈ {12, 18, 40, 100, 500, 2000}`:
261 |   the statistical threshold rises with `n` while the privacy minimum
262 |   rejects the smallest scopes.
263 | - **Roster refusal** — ~15 qualifying reports in a declared group of
264 |   `n=25`: passes the statistical gate (`15 >= 5`) and the population
265 |   minimum (`25 >= 20`), but refused because `15/25 = 0.60` exceeds
266 |   `MAX_REPORT_FRACTION` (0.5). Naming the group would name its members.
267 | - **Edge cases** — zero reports (nothing detected, nothing disclosed); a
268 |   `Report` carrying a symptom outside the fixed checklist is rejected at
269 |   construction.
270 | 
271 | Run them:
272 | 
273 | ```
274 | python -m unittest discover -s tests -v
275 | python -m microcluster.demo      # prints the scenario evaluation tables
276 | ```
277 | 
278 | ---
279 | 
280 | ## Relative detector: additive smoothing
281 | 
282 | The relative excess ratio is additively (Laplace) smoothed on both sides:
283 | 
284 | ```
285 | (reports_here + k) / (n_here + k)
286 | --------------------------------------  >= RELATIVE_EXCESS_RATIO  ->  fires
287 | (reports_control + k) / (n_control + k)
288 | ```
289 | 
290 | `k` is `config.RELATIVE_SMOOTHING` (default 1, one pseudo-report per
291 | pseudo-person). Without it, a quiet peer group pushes the control rate to
292 | almost zero and the ratio blows up into the hundreds or to infinity: a
293 | number that reads as precise but is really just division-by-noise. `k`
294 | pulls both rates toward a neutral prior, so the ratio stays finite and
295 | comparable across scopes. It is a policy constant like every other
296 | threshold here, not a derived value.
297 | 
298 | ---
299 | 
300 | ## SIMULATION
301 | 
302 | The project is expanding into an **interactive simulator**. Everything
303 | already built stays exactly as it is — `config.py`, `models.py`,
304 | `detection.py`, `disclosure.py`, `engine.py` and their passing tests are
305 | the foundation and are **not** redesigned. The simulator is new code that
306 | sits *around* them.
307 | 
308 | ### What the simulator does
309 | 
310 | 1. Simulates an illness spreading through a small shared environment — a
311 |    dorm floor of **20 to 200 people**.
312 | 2. Generates **anonymous symptom reports** from the simulated people, from
313 |    the same fixed checklist the real system uses.
314 | 3. Feeds those reports into the **existing detection engine**, unchanged.
315 | 4. Measures **how late** the detector catches the outbreak — the delay
316 |    between the outbreak actually taking hold and the detector firing.
317 | 
318 | The thesis does not move: *it detects the signal without exposing the
319 | person who created it.* The project still does **not** claim to predict
320 | real outbreaks. It is an engineering problem with a measurement attached:
321 | extract a community-level signal from sparse anonymous data without letting
322 | the signal become a privacy leak, and quantify how well that works.
323 | 
324 | ### Three outputs by submission
325 | 
326 | 1. **Live animation** of the outbreak spreading through the environment,
327 |    with the detector firing shown against it.
328 | 2. **Delay curve** — how many extra infections each additional day of
329 |    detection delay costs.
330 | 3. **Benchmark** — a classifier trained on the simulator's ground truth,
331 |    scored against the hand-authored threshold rules in `config.py` /
332 |    `disclosure.py`. The point is to see how the simple, legible policy
333 |    rules compare to a fitted model on the same data.
334 | 
335 | ### The ground-truth firewall
336 | 
337 | The simulator knows everything: who is infected, when, who infects whom.
338 | That knowledge is the simulator's alone. It is **never** passed to
339 | `detection` or `disclosure`. Those engines receive exactly what they
340 | receive in production — anonymous `Report` objects and declared scope
341 | populations — and nothing else. This is the same boundary as rule 10,
342 | extended: detection sees reports, disclosure sees aggregates, and neither
343 | ever sees the simulation's ground truth. The benchmark classifier (output
344 | 3) is the *only* consumer of ground truth, and it is scored, not wired
345 | into the disclosure path.
346 | 
347 | ### Constraints (unchanged, and binding on the simulator)
348 | 
349 | - **Fixed symptom checklist**, never free text. No natural-language
350 |   interpretation anywhere, including in the report generator.
351 | - **Clustering at category level only**, never individual-symptom
352 |   matching.
353 | - **Two detectors in parallel**: relative (versus contemporaneous peers)
354 |   and absolute (versus the configured background rate).
355 | - **Two disclosure gates, both must pass**: statistical (scales up with
356 |   `n`) and privacy (scales down with `n`).
357 | - **Every threshold is a named, configurable constant** with a comment
358 |   explaining the choice — the simulator's own parameters included.
359 | - **Privacy evaluation runs after detection, before presentation.** The
360 |   animation and the delay curve render engine output; they do not
361 |   recompute gates.
362 | - **Every emitted report is at the same `ScopeLevel` — `SUITE`.**
363 |   `detection.evaluate` raises `ValueError` on mixed-level input (its
364 |   peer-population math only holds for sibling locations). Each simulated
365 |   agent belongs to one suite; a report's `location_id` is always that
366 |   suite. The coarser scopes (`FLOOR`, `BUILDING`, `CAMPUS`) exist only in
367 |   the `ScopeRegistry` as the suite's ancestors, and are reached through
368 |   the registry, never by emitting a report against them.
369 | 
370 | ### Simulation parameters are chosen, not measured
371 | 
372 | Transmission probability, incubation distribution, reporting propensity,
373 | contact structure, background symptom rate — every one of these is a
374 | **chosen** figure, picked to produce a legible demonstration, not measured
375 | from real data. **Anything user-facing must say so**, in the same plain
376 | language `config.py` uses for `BACKGROUND_RATE`. The simulator's constants
377 | live in their own config module with the same naming and commenting
378 | discipline as `microcluster/config.py`.
379 | 
380 | ### Timeline
381 | 
382 | - **Idea locked: September 2.** No concept evaluation before October 6.
383 | - **Feature freeze: September 25.**
384 | - **Submission: October 6.**
385 | 
386 | ---
387 | 
388 | ## SECURITY POSTURE
389 | 
390 | - No accounts, no passwords, no sessions, no external API keys, no
391 |   uploads, no SQL, no free text. Most standard web vulnerabilities are
392 |   **structurally absent, not mitigated**. There is no auth to bypass, no
393 |   injection sink, no file handler, no session to fixate.
394 | - Input validation is **whitelist-based** via the fixed checklist
395 |   (`models.py`). A `Report` whose category, onset, or symptom is not an
396 |   exact member of the closed vocabulary is rejected at construction.
397 | - The privacy strategy is **DATA MINIMIZATION, not encryption**. The
398 |   system never holds identity, free text, precise time, or precise
399 |   location, so there is very little to encrypt. You cannot leak what you
400 |   did not collect.
401 | - **CRITICAL for the future API layer:** detection and disclosure MUST
402 |   run server side. Raw `Report` objects must NEVER be sent to the client.
403 |   The API returns `ScopeEvaluation` records (and the detection findings)
404 |   only. Doing gate evaluation or scope selection in the client would make
405 |   the privacy gate bypassable with devtools, network inspection, or a
406 |   patched bundle.
407 | - Anonymity was chosen over identity-based abuse prevention. Per-session
408 |   and per-day submission caps (`SESSION_SUBMISSION_CAP`,
409 |   `DAILY_SUBMISSION_CAP`) are the only mitigation, and this is
410 |   deliberate: identity is the thing the system exists to protect, so we
411 |   will not introduce it to stop abuse.
412 | 
413 | ---
414 | 
415 | ## UI CONSTRAINTS (binding when the frontend is built)
416 | 
417 | There is no frontend yet. When one is built, these are binding.
418 | 
419 | **Prohibited:** gradients, Lucide icons, pure white backgrounds, rainbow
420 | coloring, drop shadows, three-feature-card rows, emoji, glassmorphism, em
421 | dashes in copy, Inter / Geist / Space Grotesk, colored left stripes, fake
422 | testimonials, bento grids, terminal window mockups, "it's not X, it's Y"
423 | phrasing, checkmark bullets, three pricing tiers, soft corner radii,
424 | purple-and-black, radial orbs, dot grids, sparkle icons, animated arrows,
425 | neon colors, basic pastels.
426 | 
427 | **Required:** real working product demos (not mockups); skeleton loaders
428 | for async states; a Terms of Service page; and a privacy policy that
429 | states the ACTUAL mechanism (what is stored, what is never collected, how
430 | the thresholds work) rather than boilerplate.
431 | 
432 | **Visual direction:** flat, high contrast, documentary / clinical.
433 | Off-white or light gray background. Fonts limited to the system UI stack,
434 | IBM Plex, Source Sans, or a serif such as Charter.
435 | 
436 | **Motion (amended after the Week-4 freeze):** the rule used to be "no
437 | transitions, no animation," full stop. Changed because the shipped UI was
438 | correct but inert -- nothing moved until a judge clicked a button they
439 | might not click, which is worse than the gradients/glassmorphism/emoji
440 | this list exists to keep out. Motion is now permitted ONLY where it:
441 | 
442 | - carries a state change already happening in the simulation (an agent's
443 |   colour changing because its infection state changed; a gate table row's
444 |   background flashing, once, because its PASS/FAIL changed; the refusal
445 |   box or status line settling in when what they say changes), or
446 | - gives ordinary interaction feedback (hover/focus background and border
447 |   on a control).
448 | 
449 | Every transition or animation is capped at 400ms, plays once, and never
450 | loops -- no `infinite`, no continuously repeating `@keyframes`. The one
451 | purely decorative exception, and the only one permitted anywhere, is a
452 | single ~350ms monochrome ring on the instant a circle turns symptomatic
453 | during real playback. Every rule above must be inert under
454 | `prefers-reduced-motion: reduce`. "Hover animations" is removed from the
455 | prohibited list above for exactly this narrow case; it is not open to
456 | further motion beyond what is described here. Gradients, shadows,
457 | glassmorphism/blur, and anything that loops remain banned without
458 | exception, and `tests/test_motion_constraints.py` enforces that.
